## 1. Purpose

After T08, everything a player reads or touches outside the canvas exists, looks like part of the same 80s world, and can be proven not to slow the frame. Observable outcomes:

1. A title screen over the live world: a chrome wordmark built from system fonts and CSS only, a prompt, controls, and best scores. The first real gesture launches, inside the browser's activation window, so audio can start. Nothing launches or creates audio before that.
2. A neon HUD with score, SYNC, shield pips, overdrive, km, sector and objective (§7.5), usable and uncrowded at phone, tablet, desktop and ultrawide sizes. The executor has opened and described screenshots at each size.
3. A cruise menu that says the road never stops (§7.2), operable by keyboard alone, mouse alone and touch alone.
4. Overlays for SIGNAL LOST, autopilot and context loss, and an opaque fatal page for each of the four `FatalReason` values.
5. Keyboard, mouse and touch produce `ControlFrame` samples per §7.3: WASD and arrows rate-steer, the mouse is an absolute target, touch is a relative drag, Space, left button, second finger and an on-screen button boost.
6. Measured frame cost: zero DOM writes when nothing changes, a counted budget when things do, no layout from bar movement, and no sampled allocation in `ui.frame` or `input.sample` on unchanged frames.
7. Accessibility basics: real roles and names, visible focus, a focus-trapped menu, 44 px touch targets, measured text contrast, and no motion under reduced motion.

"Looks good" is judged by viewing images. The gates in section 7 catch regressions and do not establish it (§11.5).

### Amendment FH, 2026-11-07: FULLSCREEN and HIDE HUD (operator request, awaiting review)

The operator asked, while reviewing on a phone and a tablet: "Two feature requests I want to implment while I'm reviewing. A fullscreen and a hide the HUD options you can select from the pause menu." The pause menu is the CRUISE card. What a player sees after FH:

8. **FULLSCREEN**, a checkbox in the CRUISE card. Ticking it puts the whole page, road, HUD and menu, in the browser's fullscreen; unticking it leaves. The box always shows whether the page really is fullscreen, however fullscreen ended: Esc, the browser's own control, a swipe or the OS. Where the browser offers no element fullscreen, as Safari on iPhone [M], the row is not there. It is not remembered between visits, because a browser enters fullscreen only from a tap, click or key. In fullscreen, Esc belongs to the browser: it leaves fullscreen, and a run that was in PLAY is then in CRUISE, so after Esc the player is in the menu, as Esc outside fullscreen would put them. In CRUISE, Esc in fullscreen leaves fullscreen and the menu stays open.
9. **HIDE HUD**, a checkbox in the CRUISE card, remembered with the other settings. In PLAY it clears the readouts off the road: score, SYNC, distance, sector, objective, shields, overdrive, the touch BOOST button, the MUTED chip, the score popups and the sector banner. What stays: II, because on a touch screen it is the only way back to the menu; SIGNAL LOST, the autopilot tag, the context-lost layer and the fatal page, which say what state the game is in; and the screen-reader live region. A second finger still boosts. The title screen does not change.
10. The card gains one row, FULLSCREEN, HIDE HUD, REDUCED MOTION, aligned with the quality radios, and still fits at every size A9 checks without scrolling, as before. Nothing else about the look changes (T11 is waiting on the operator's verdict of the look).

**FH review round 1 (FH-R1), 2026-11-07.** The first review asked for changes and approved nothing. Eight findings are answered in place below, each marked `FH-R1 (n)` with the superseded text struck through, not deleted; the revision note in section 14 lists them. Purpose items 8 to 10 do not change.

### Amendment LH, 2026-11-11: LAUNCH with or without the HUD (operator request, approved in session)

The operator asked: "I want to let the user to start the game with or without the hud. Change the start button to two buttons, one to start with the hud and the other without the hud. Keep the option in the cruise menu so the user can switch." What a player sees after LH:

11. **Two start buttons on the title**, **LAUNCH** and **LAUNCH - NO HUD**, in place of the one PRESS ANY KEY / TAP TO LAUNCH button. LAUNCH starts a run with the HUD shown; LAUNCH - NO HUD starts it with the HUD hidden exactly as FH's HIDE HUD hides it. The choice is the same remembered setting as the CRUISE card's HIDE HUD box, which stays and still switches it during a run.
12. **Any key, or a tap or click on the title away from the buttons, still launches**, with the remembered choice. At load the focus sits on the button that matches it, so a key press launches "the focused button", and Tab reaches the other.

### Amendment WM, 2026-11-12: wordmark rim without inner lines (operator request, approved in session)

The operator sent a phone screenshot of the title: "The letters appear to be formed from overlapping rectangles. You can see the outlines inside the letters. The M D H T V E T R each show the issue." What a player sees after WM:

13. **The MIDNIGHT VECTOR wordmark shows no lines inside its letters** in any font the stack falls back to, while keeping the thin white rim, the chrome gradient and the glow.
14. **The wordmark keeps its chrome under a browser's dark theme for sites.** A second operator report, with a phone screenshot beside an agent's: "on your screenshot the title has a chrome look to it. On my phone it is black instead of the chrome look."

## 2. Context and orientation

Working directory for every command: `C:\highsierralabs\terrain`. `§n` means a section of `architecture.md`. Not a git repository. T00 must be `done` first: until then `src/contracts`, `src/core`, `src/testing` and the npm scripts do not exist.

Terms:

- **Activating event:** a browser event that grants transient user activation, the state in which `AudioContext` may start. Which events count is measured in section 10.
- **Slot:** a small object in `src/ui/slots.ts` that owns one text node or one style property, remembers the last value written, and writes only on change.
- **DOM write:** any mutation of the UI subtree or any `style.setProperty` call. The `writes:` gate counts them.
- **Stage:** in the dev page, the element standing in for the canvas. It carries a CSS backdrop and receives `input.attach`.
- **Play window:** the x and y range in §5.3, `PLAY_HALF_X`, `PLAY_Y_MIN`, `PLAY_Y_MAX`.

**Where the evidence comes from.** Every `[O]` below was run on 2026-11-05 in a throwaway scratchpad against the reference install of the §2 pins, Chromium revision 1234 through Playwright 1.64.1, on the RTX 4070 SUPER machine. Mock HTML, not project code, was shot and viewed. It is not part of the project, none of it counts as acceptance, and the executor reruns everything. Values with no tag, and all CSS numbers marked `[A]`, are starting points to iterate on.

**Module rules that shape this plan** (§3.2, T00's lint table): `src/ui` and `src/input` may import `src/contracts` and `src/core`, and nothing else, including each other. Neither may import `three` or `postprocessing`. So the two modules T08 owns still only meet through contracts, the DOM and `app/`.

**Amendment FH, orientation.** T08 is reopened by the T00 owner, the orchestrator acting as the operator's delegate, for an operator-requested scope addition; the evidence for the choices below is in section 10 under "FH planning probes". Terms:

- **Element fullscreen:** the Fullscreen API on an element, here `document.documentElement`. Two forms exist: the standard one (`document.fullscreenEnabled`, `Element.requestFullscreen()` returning a promise, `document.fullscreenElement`, `document.exitFullscreen()`, the `fullscreenchange` and `fullscreenerror` events) and the `webkit`-prefixed one (`webkitFullscreenEnabled`, `webkitRequestFullscreen()` returning nothing, `webkitFullscreenElement`, `webkitExitFullscreen()`, `webkitfullscreenchange`, `webkitfullscreenerror`), which iPadOS Safari before 16.4 needs [M]. TypeScript 6's `lib.dom` types only the standard form [O: `node_modules/typescript/lib/lib.dom.d.ts`], so the prefixed one is reached through a local structural type.
- **Own exit:** fullscreen ending because the FULLSCREEN box asked. **Leaving:** fullscreen ending any other way: Esc, the browser's control, a swipe, the OS, a page script.
- **The operator's review build:** the operator plays `dist/` through their own `vite preview` on port 5100, over Tailscale. This task never writes `dist/`, never uses port 5100 or 5173, and stops only processes it started. So FH reaches the operator's devices only when the operator or the T00 owner rebuilds `dist/` (section 13, FH).
- **Headless limits that shape the tests** [O: section 10]: Chromium 1234 under Playwright keeps the emulated viewport size in fullscreen, and a synthetic Esc reaches the page without leaving fullscreen. So the tests use a page-script exit as the stand-in for a browser's own exit, and `setViewportSize` as the stand-in for a screen larger than the window; the real behaviour is a human check. **FH-R1 (8):** because the viewport keeps its size, "equals the viewport" holds whether or not the page is fullscreen, so no browser row here shows that anything fills a real screen. They show only that the API engaged (`fullscreenElement` is `<html>`) and that layout follows a resize while fullscreen stays on. Fill on a real screen is HC8 (phone), HC9 (tablet) and HC11 (desktop).
- **FH-R1, the order of a fullscreen change** [O: probe P5, section 10]: a request or an exit settles its promise first, with `fullscreenElement` already set or cleared, and the change event fires later, at the next rendering step. A refused request rejects first and fires `fullscreenerror` after. So a test that waits on `fullscreenElement` alone can act before the product has seen the event; every FH test waits on an event counter instead (FH-27).

## 4. Interfaces

**Consumed**, cited from `src/contracts/index.ts`: `ControlFrame`, `InputSource`, `CreateInput`, `Ui`, `UiCommands`, `FatalReason`, `CreateUi`, `FrameView`, `SimMode`, `Ev`, `EventBuffer`, `Objective`, `Tier`, `PALETTE_FLOATS`, `PALETTE_NAMES`, `BIOME_NAMES`, `SHIELD_MAX`, `SYNC_MAX`, `CHUNK_LEN`. From `src/core/coords.ts`: `between`. For dev and test only: `src/testing/scriptedFrame.ts` (injected into the dev page, never imported by `src/ui`), and `test`, `expect`, `saveShot` from `tests/support/pw.ts`. T08 does not use `openDevPage` or `expectRasterizer`, because `dev/ui.html` has no dev shell and no WebGL.

**Provided:**

```ts
// src/input/index.ts -- the only export
export const createInput: CreateInput;
// src/ui/index.ts -- the only export
export const createUi: CreateUi;
```

**`InputSource` behaviour.** `attach(el)` sets `el.style.touchAction = 'none'`, listens for pointer events on `el`, for `keydown`, `keyup` and `blur` on `window`, for `visibilitychange` on `document`, and for delegated boost-button pointer events on `document` in the capture phase. `detach()` removes all of it through one `AbortController` and restores `touchAction`. Both are idempotent, and `sample(out)` writes the five fields and allocates nothing.

| Source | Output |
|---|---|
| Steer key held (`KeyW`, `KeyA`, `KeyS`, `KeyD`, arrows) | `absolute: false`. `moveX` is `-1`, `0` or `1`, `moveY` likewise, up positive. Opposite keys cancel. Diagonals are not normalised, because the play window is anisotropic |
| Mouse moved, no steer key held | `absolute: true`. `moveX = clamp((px / w * 2 - 1) / 0.8, -1, 1)` and `moveY = clamp((1 - py / h * 2) / 0.8, -1, 1)`, so the central 80% of the element covers the whole window [A] |
| One finger or pen dragging | `absolute: true`, from a persistent virtual target. Each pointer delta adds `2 * dx / (0.6 * minDim)` to x and `-2 * dy / (0.6 * minDim)` to y, in CSS px, clamped to `[-1, 1]`, with `minDim = min(w, h)` [A]. Finger down moves nothing |
| Steer key released after keyboard use | `absolute: false`, `0, 0`, so the ship stays put instead of flying back to an old mouse target, until the pointer moves again |
| No device used yet | `absolute: false`, `0, 0` |
| `boost` | Space held, or left mouse button held on `el`, or two or more touches on `el`, or a pointer held on `[data-mv-boost]` |
| `human` | True when any handled event arrived since the last sample, or a key, button or finger is currently held. A resting mouse is false |

A `keydown` on a focused control keeps that control's own keys and steers with the rest (`keepsKey` in `keys.ts`, VI repair): a button or checkbox keeps only Space and Enter, a radio also keeps its arrows, and a slider, text field, select or editable text keeps every key. The focus is read when input handles the key, so `src/ui`'s hand-back below takes effect in the same dispatch. In PLAY, a steering key or Space while II has focus blurs II in the capture phase, and Space's press of the button is cancelled, so steering goes on and Space boosts at once. `keyup` is never filtered. Handled keys on other targets call `preventDefault`, so the page cannot scroll. `blur` and hidden state clear every held key, button and finger. `contextmenu` on `el` and on the boost button is prevented, so a long press cannot open a menu.

**When `Ui` calls `UiCommands`:**

| Command | Called |
|---|---|
| `launch()` | Synchronously inside `pointerup` (mouse button 0, or any touch or pen) or `click` on the title overlay, or `keydown` passing `isLaunchKey`, while the last mode seen is DEMO and `html[data-mv-boot]` reads `running` or is absent (DI-95, `bootReady`). May be called twice for one gesture (`pointerup`, then `click`) |
| `togglePause()` | Non-repeat `keydown` of Esc or P, the HUD menu button and the Resume button, only when the last mode seen is PLAY or CRUISE |
| `setVolume(m, s)` | Always once at mount. On every slider `input` event. On M, which sends `0, 0` or restores the stored values |
| `setQuality(t)` | At mount only if the stored value is not `'auto'`. On radio change |
| `setReducedMotion(on)` | Always once at mount. On checkbox change. On an OS media-query change when the player has set no override |

**Warm panels (D-012, DI-89).** During DEMO the HUD and the cruise card are laid out and drawn under the title, each on its own layer (`will-change: opacity`), at opacity .001, with `pointer-events: none`, `inert` and `aria-hidden="true"`; the menu's scrim is not drawn then. The launch, and the first Esc, change only what is already drawn. `inert` and `aria-hidden` are written in the same step as `data-view` (`index.ts`, `createReach`). Every other overlay, the context-lost layer among them, is still hidden by `display`, so T09's B12 holds. **For T10 and T11:** Playwright counts opacity .001 as visible, so `[data-mv="hud"]` and `[data-mv="cruise"]` read `toBeVisible()` in DEMO; to ask whether a player sees them, read `data-view`, `aria-hidden` or the computed opacity.

**`Ui` behaviour.** `mount(root, cmd)` adds class `mv-ui` to `root`, injects the stylesheet and builds the tree, in the title state, before any frame. `frame(v)` never retains `v`. Which panels show comes only from `v.sim.mode`, `v.sim.autopilot` and the context-lost flag, so UI and sim cannot disagree about the mode. `showFatal(r)` works before `mount`, by appending to `document.body`; the first reason wins, repeats are ignored, it never throws, and later `frame` calls do nothing. Timers use `v.tick`, never `setTimeout`, so a scripted frame reproduces a state.

**DOM hooks** T10 and T11 may rely on, as `data-mv` attribute values: `title`, `wordmark`, `launch`, `best`, `hud`, `score`, `sync`, `shields`, `overdrive`, `km`, `sector`, `objective`, `menu-button`, `mute`, `autopilot`, `popups`, `banner`, `signal-lost`, `context-lost`, `cruise`, `resume`, `music`, `sfx`, `quality`, `reduced`, `fatal`, `reload`, `live`, plus `boost` and `mute-toggle` (the menu's checkbox; `mute` is the HUD chip). The boost button additionally has `data-mv-boost`. `<html>` gets `data-mv-mode="demo|play|cruise|lost"`, used to hide the cursor over `canvas` during play.

**Persistent keys** (`localStorage`, all access in try/catch): `mv.bests.v1` is `{"score":n,"km":n}`. `mv.settings.v1` is `{"music":0.8,"sfx":0.8,"muted":false,"quality":"auto","reduced":null}`, where `quality` is `"auto"` or 0 to 4, and `reduced` null means follow the OS.

**Seams.** The types alone do not settle these. All were compared at reconciliation, and where a row and §8 differ, §8 wins.

| Other task | Topic | T08's assumption |
|---|---|---|
| T02 | `ControlFrame` meaning | As §8.1's comment. `absolute: false`: rates in `[-1, 1]`. `absolute: true`: target in `[-1, 1]` across the play window, `x = moveX * PLAY_HALF_X`, `y = PLAY_Y_MIN + (moveY + 1) / 2 * (PLAY_Y_MAX - PLAY_Y_MIN)`. +X is sim +x and screen right; +Y is up. The sim approaches a target at its own bounded rate and never snaps, because a stale touch target can sit far from the ship after autopilot |
| T02, T10 | `human` and idle | `human` is true for a frame with any device event, or while anything is held. T02's 10 s idle timer (§7.2) resets on it. `human` is not a launch signal: only `cmd.launch()` launches. A control frame in DEMO, CRUISE or SIGNAL_LOST is ignored by the sim, not treated as an error |
| T02 | `boost` | Level, not edge. The sim decides whether boost is allowed. Input does not know the mode or the overdrive level |
| T02 | Scales | `score` non-negative and integer-valued, 0 until `launch`, reset only by `launch`. `sync` integer 1 to `SYNC_MAX`, `shields` integer 0 to `SHIELD_MAX`, `overdrive` in `[0, 1]`, `ghost` seconds remaining, above 0 only during a ghost |
| T02 | Autopilot flag | The UI shows the tag only when `mode === PLAY && autopilot`. T02 may set `autopilot` as it likes in other modes |
| T02 | Events | As §8.1's comment above `Ev`. `value`: SHARD, GATE, GRAZE points added including multipliers, 0 when cosmetic; HIT shields remaining; SECTOR new index; GRADE bonus points; others 0. `index` on GRADE: rank, 0 S, 1 A, 2 B, 3 C. Popups are cosmetic, and `src/ui/events.ts` is the only reader. Shields and score are read from `SimState` and the sector from `v.zone`, never events, because overflow may drop events |
| T02, T01 | Objective units | `zone.objectiveTarget` and `sim.objectiveProgress` share a unit: a count for GATES, CHAIN, GRAZES; u for NO_HIT and BOOST. Progress restarts when the sector changes. Display clamps progress to target |
| T02 | `sectorIndex` | The sector shown reads `v.zone.index`, and the palette name reads the same object. `sim.sectorIndex` can lead by one frame |
| T02 | Hit-stop | `feel.shipHold > 0` means §7.4's 70 ms hold is active. The UI freezes readout text and bars during it, not panels or popups |
| T02, T10 | Bests | §7.1's localStorage bests live in T08, because `sim` is pure and has no storage. T02 and T10 do not touch those keys. HUD km, the menu's run line and furthest are one number: the distance travelled while score accrues, which is `mode === PLAY && !autopilot`, the sim's own test for live rules. It starts at the launch, not chunk 0, since DEMO can run for hours, and it holds through CRUISE, idle autopilot and SIGNAL_LOST (§7.2, DI-61) |
| T09 | Warp | Once `sim.warpSerial !== 0` the UI never writes bests, so a harness run cannot poison them. HUD formats stay within their box at 1e15 u. The UI has no timers, so `manual(true)` simply stops it |
| T01 | Palette and biome order | `paletteA` indexes §6.8's rows top to bottom, `biomeA` indexes §5.4's rows top to bottom, as `PALETTE_NAMES` and `BIOME_NAMES` in §8.1. `zone.palette` is 21 linear floats; grid at offset 15, accent at 18, per §8.1. It is read only when its quantised key changes |
| T07 | Volume | `setVolume(music, sfx)` takes linear gains in `[0, 1]`, may be called before `unlock()` and must be remembered. Mute is `setVolume(0, 0)`, so T07 needs no mute concept |
| T07, T10 | Audio unlock | T08 guarantees `cmd.launch()` runs synchronously inside an activating event. T08 does not retry after the title is gone. §10.5's retry on a later gesture belongs to T07 (§8.1). Touch `pointerdown` is not activating [O], so nobody may unlock there |
| T06 | Quality | `setQuality(t)` means `governor.force(t)`. Menu labels follow §6.10: 4 High, 3 Standard, 2 Low, 1 Minimum, 0 Software. `v.quality.tier` is what the menu shows beside AUTO |
| T10 | Commands | `launch`: `sim.command('launch')` and `audio.unlock()` synchronously in the call, idempotent, no-op outside DEMO. `togglePause`: PLAY to `pause`, CRUISE to `resume`, otherwise nothing. `setReducedMotion`: `sim.setReducedMotion(on)` and `view.reducedMotion = on`. `ui.mount` runs after audio, sim and view exist, and before the renderer and the final governor. `setQuality` at mount is buffered by T10 |
| T10 | Reduced motion source | T08 is the only reader of `prefers-reduced-motion`. T10 starts false and receives the value at mount. T10 and other layers read `v.reducedMotion` |
| T10 | Elements | `input.attach(canvas)`, not a wrapper. `ui.mount(root, cmd)`, with `root` an empty element above the canvas, not inside a transformed ancestor. The UI's own CSS makes `root` `position: fixed; inset: 0; pointer-events: none`, and only interactive parts re-enable pointer events, so canvas pointer input passes through the HUD and a launch click never reaches the canvas |
| T10 | `index.html` | `<html lang="en">`, a `<title>`, `<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width,initial-scale=1,viewport-fit=cover">` (needed for `env(safe-area-inset-*)` and phone metrics), body margin 0 and `overflow: hidden`, `overscroll-behavior: none`. The UI sets no `html` or `body` rule beyond `data-mv-mode` |
| T10 | Fatal | T10 stops the loop and may remove the canvas after `ui.showFatal(r)`. The page is opaque either way. T10's 15 s restore timer calls `showFatal('context-lost-permanent')`. `no-webgl2` from T08 appears only if `new WebGLRenderer` throws after the §10.1 pre-check passed; the poster case stays T10's, and its copy should not contradict `copy.ts` |
| T10 | Frame order | `ui.frame(v)` in step 13 sees this frame's `v.events` before step 14 clears them. `input.sample` in step 3 runs every frame in every mode |
| T10 | Context-lost overlay | `showContextLost(true)` on loss, `false` on restore. `ui.frame` keeps being called during loss. It does not block input or the title |
| T10 | Boot state (DI-95) | T08 reads T10's `html[data-mv-boot]`, which `main.ts` sets to `loading` before it fetches the app and the loop sets to `running` on the first frame. Until it reads `running` the prompt shows `LOADING` (CSS on the attribute) and `tryLaunch` ignores input. No attribute at all, as on `dev/ui.html`, means no boot to wait for. `failed` and `unsupported` also hold the launch. No T10 change |
| T10 | II under a fine pointer (VI-game) | In PLAY under `(pointer: fine)`, II has `pointer-events: none`, so a mouse press there reaches the canvas as boost. A mouse can no longer open CRUISE from II in PLAY; Esc, P, and Tab then Enter do. **T10's A19 row "menu button opens CRUISE" clicks II with a mouse on the `gpu` project and now fails; it is T10's to change** (section 13) |
| T11 | Look | T11's temporary ownership does not include `src/ui/**`. A UI retune reopens this plan. Palette tints follow the zone automatically |
| T00 | Test helpers | `test`, `expect` and `saveShot` from `tests/support/pw.ts` work on a page with no `window.__shell`. The auto fixture adds no `evaluate` before a test's own actions, because that would grant activation and void the gesture tests; the Escape control in `gesture:` proves this |
| T00 | `scriptedFrame` | Imports no three. Returns one object whose `sim` and `zone` are plain mutable objects at run time, so `installDevPage` can edit them between `ui.frame` calls without calling `scriptedFrame` inside a measured loop. `at` drives `zone.index`, and `objective` is `index % 5` |
| T00 | Tooling | `tsconfig.json` includes `src/ui/*.ts` with `vite/client` types, so `./ui.css?inline` type-checks. Vitest does not need that import, since unit tests never load `style.ts`. `tests/visual/**` may import `src/ui/devPage.ts` for types |

**Amendment FH, interfaces.** No contract change: `Ui`, `UiCommands` and every §8 type stay as they are (section 12, FH).

- **DOM hooks added:** `fullscreen` and `hide-hud`, the two new checkboxes, additive like `mute-toggle`. The FULLSCREEN label carries `hidden` where element fullscreen is unavailable. Root (`.mv-ui`) gains `data-hide-hud="0|1"`, written at mount and on change only, never per frame.
- **Persistent keys:** `mv.settings.v1` becomes `{"music":0.8,"sfx":0.8,"muted":false,"quality":"auto","reduced":null,"hideHud":false}`. No version bump: the field is additive, a stored object without it loads `hideHud: false`, and `loadSettings` reads fields by name, so an object with the extra field would load the same in the code before FH [I: by reading `loadSettings`]. Fullscreen is not stored.
- **When `Ui` calls `UiCommands`, additions to the table above:** `togglePause()` is also called once from `frame(v)`, on the first frame after fullscreen ended by anything but the FULLSCREEN box, when that frame's `v.sim.mode` is PLAY; the flag is consumed on that frame whatever the mode. Esc no longer calls `togglePause()` while either API reports a fullscreen element. P, II and Resume are unchanged. ~~T10's `togglePause` reads the sim's mode at call time and only queues a command [O: `src/app/commands.ts`], so a call from inside `ui.frame` needs nothing from T10.~~ **FH-V1:** T10's `togglePause` reads the sim's mode at call time and calls `sim.command`, which applies at once [O: `src/app/commands.ts`, `src/sim/sim.ts` `command` through `applyCommand`, `src/sim/rules.ts` `command()`, read-only]. `FrameView.sim` is that live state (`src/app/boot.ts` line 48), so a call from inside `ui.frame` changes `v.sim.mode` and `v.sim.autopilot` before the call returns. `frame` therefore makes the call before it reads the mode, so the rest of that frame draws CRUISE (FH-36). It needs nothing from T10.
- **Fullscreen target:** `document.documentElement`, so `#mv-stage`, `#mv-ui` and the fatal page stay inside it.

| Other task | Topic | T08's FH assumption |
|---|---|---|
| T10 | Fill in fullscreen | `#mv-stage`, its canvas and `#mv-ui` keep filling the viewport with `<html>` in fullscreen and follow a resize there [O: section 10, probe P3 on T10's code]. The app row A31 reads it, read-only |
| T10 | `panelState(page, 'hud')` in `tests/e2e/lib/app.ts` | Reads the HUD element, which stays shown with HIDE HUD on, while its panels are `visibility: hidden`. No e2e sets HIDE HUD [grep], so nothing changes; a later test that asks whether readouts show reads `.mv-ui[data-hide-hud]` |
| T10 | A19 rows in `tests/e2e/modes.spec.ts` | They reach II, Resume, REDUCED MOTION and the quality radios by `data-mv` and live boxes, so the new row does not move what they read. Rerun read-only (A37) |
| T11 | `ui-cruise` sheet from `tests/art/play.spec.ts` | It photographs the CRUISE card and asserts nothing about its contents; its existing images show the card before FH, without the new row. T11's to reshoot, not T08's to run (section 13, FH) |

## 5. Plan of work

Milestones run in order and each can be verified alone. Input first, because it has no visual dependency. All visual milestones include viewing images, and each viewed image gets one line in Progress saying what was seen.

### M0. Preconditions

**Goal:** the ground is real. **Work:** confirm T00 is `done`. Read `src/contracts/index.ts`: section 12's requests are all in §8. Run `npm run check:task -- T08`. **Result:** known starting point. **Proof:** the command exits 1 with `VACUOUS`, the fail-before state.

### M1. Input core

**Goal:** all of §7.3's logic, testable in Node. **Work:** `core.ts` exports `createInputCore()`, with methods `key(code, down)`, `pointerDown(id, type, button, x, y)`, `pointerMove(id, type, x, y)`, `pointerUp(id)`, `boostButton(id, down)`, `resize(w, h)`, `clear()`, `sample(out)`. Hold finger IDs in a fixed array of 5. No DOM type appears here. A first finger steers. A second counts toward boost. If the first lifts while the second stays, the second becomes the steer finger with no jump and boost ends. `resize(0, 0)` must not produce NaN. **Result:** the source table in section 4 holds. **Proof:** A1, A1n.

### M2. Input binding and the dev page

**Goal:** real browser events reach the core. **Work:** `src/input/index.ts`. Cache the element rect at attach and on `ResizeObserver`; never read layout in a handler. Use `KeyboardEvent.code` for steering and boost. Create `dev/ui.html` with exactly this script, so the page stays thin and all logic is type-checked:

```html
<script type="module">
  import { createUi } from '/src/ui/index.ts';
  import { createInput } from '/src/input/index.ts';
  import { scriptedFrame } from '/src/testing/scriptedFrame.ts';
  import { installDevPage } from '/src/ui/devPage.ts';
  installDevPage({ createUi, createInput, scriptedFrame });
</script>
```

`installDevPage` receives implementations by injection, so `src/ui/devPage.ts` imports only contracts and passes lint. It runs synchronously to completion, so the page is ready when `page.goto` returns. **This matters:** a polling wait before the first gesture would use `evaluate`, which grants activation and voids the gesture tests.

`dev/ui.html` also holds, inline: the viewport meta, `<link rel="icon" href="data:,">`, `<div id="stage">` with the CSS backdrop (sky gradient, banded sun, perspective grid) and `<div id="ui">`.

`DevUi` on `window.__ui`: `calls` (every `UiCommands` call in order), `frame(t, o)`, `state(name)`, `sample()`, `ui`, `input`, `view` (the last `FrameView`), and `allocProbe(n)` (a deliberate allocator, the negative control for `alloc:`).

Query parameters: `?state=<name>`, `?bg=scene|white|black`, `?palette=0..5`, `?mount=0` (no mount), `?audioprobe=1` (the `launch` stub creates an `AudioContext` and records its state, and a capture-phase `keydown` listener does the same on Escape, as the control), `?audioprobe=early` (also creates one at load and records it).

**Result:** the page runs with a stub UI. **Proof:** A3.

### M3. UI pure logic

**Goal:** formats, models and storage settled before any pixels. **Work:**

- `format.ts`. `formatScore`: floor, `padStart(8, '0')` below 1e10, otherwise `toExponential(3).toUpperCase()`, and a non-finite value gives `--------`. `formatKm(u)`: floor to tenths below 1e6 km, otherwise exponent form. Maximum 9 characters, never a locale API. `formatSync(n)`: `×n`. `formatSector(i, palette)`: `S07 ULTRAVIOLET`. `formatObjective(kind, progress, target)`: `GATES 6/10`, `CHAIN 6/10`, `GRAZES 6/10`, `CLEAN 1.2/3.2 KM`, `BOOST 1.2/3.2 KM`, plus ` OK` when done.
- `slots.ts`. `createTextSlot(target, format, minTicks = 6)`: `set(tick, value)` compares a number first, formats only on change, writes `target.data`, and writes at most once per 6 ticks (50 ms). A pending value always lands on a later frame. A tick that goes backwards resets the throttle. **Every slot, style and attribute included, uses this one throttle** (DI-50), leading-edge: a change after 6 or more quiet ticks writes at once, so a beat kick in normal play gets no added delay, and only a value changing faster than that waits. `createAttrSlot(target, name, table, minTicks = 6)` is the attribute form. `createStyleSlot(target, prop, table)`: index into a prebuilt string table. Overdrive uses 129 prebuilt `scaleX(i/128)` strings, built once at mount, so bar updates allocate nothing and hot paths have no template strings (`Agents.md`).
- `view.ts`: bit mask of TITLE, HUD, MENU, LOST, AUTO, CTX.
- `palette.ts`: read grid and accent from `v.zone.palette`, convert to sRGB, lift by mixing toward white in linear light until luminance is at least 0.40 [A, see section 10]. Only recomputed when `paletteA`, `paletteB` or `floor(paletteMix * 16)` changes, so a 20 s crossfade costs 32 style writes.
- `run.ts` (R1 repair, DI-61): `createRunMeter().frame(mode, autopilot, travel, out)` writes this run's distance to `out[0]`. DEMO resets it. Each scoring spell is one exact `between` from where the spell began, added to what earlier spells banked, so CRUISE, idle autopilot and SIGNAL_LOST hold it and a resume continues with no jump.
- `storage.ts`: `StoreLike` is `{ getItem, setItem }`. Parse, validate, clamp, default. Bests only rise. `shouldRecord(warpSerial)`. Bests are written at most once per 600 ticks while they are rising, and on `pagehide`, never per score change (DI-52); settings persist on every change.
- `events.ts`: `Ev` to popup. SHARD gives none, since at 12.5 per second it would dominate the write budget. GATE `+<value>`, GRAZE `GRAZE +<value>`, GATE_MISS `MISS`, HIT `HIT`, GRADE `GRADE <letter>`. A GATE or GRAZE with `value` 0 is cosmetic (§8.1) and makes no popup, so an idle frame never shows `+0`.
- `gesture.ts`: `isLaunchKey` is false for Tab, Shift, Control, Alt, Meta, Escape, CapsLock, NumLock, ScrollLock, ContextMenu, PrintScreen, Pause, F1 to F12, any `Audio*`, `Browser*`, `Media*` code, `KeyM`, `KeyP`, and any combination with Ctrl, Alt or Meta. True otherwise.

**Result:** logic tested with no browser. **Proof:** A2.

### M4. Skeleton, stylesheet, title and wordmark

**Goal:** the first look, the part the player sees before anything else. **Work:** `dom.ts`, `style.ts`, `title.ts`, `index.ts`, `ui.css`.

**Stylesheet delivery:** `import css from './ui.css?inline'` and one `<style>` element. No separate request, so the fatal page works even after a failed load [O: the build emitted no CSS file].

**Tokens, starting values:**

| Token | Value | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `--mv-u` | `clamp(11px, calc(.5vw + .75vh), 20px)`; every size is an `em` of it | Gave 11 px on phones, 12 to 13 on tablets, 17.7 at 1080p, 20 at 3440x1440 [O] |
| `--mv-mono` | `"Courier New", ui-monospace, monospace`, weight 700 | §7.5 |
| `--mv-display` | `"Arial Black", "Segoe UI Black", Impact, Roboto, "Helvetica Neue", system-ui, sans-serif`, weight 900, wordmark only | Decision log; fallback on phones untested [M] |
| `--mv-accent`, `--mv-hot` | From the palette, defaults `#2DE2E6` and `#FF7FD0` | Lifted |
| `--mv-sun`, `--mv-text` | `#FFD319`, `#EAF6FF` | [A] |
| `--mv-panel` | `rgba(10, 3, 30, .8)` | Contrast held at a flat .8 and failed with a .62 to .42 gradient [O] |

**Wordmark:** two lines, MIDNIGHT then VECTOR, `h1` with accessible name "Midnight Vector". Upright glyphs with `transform: skewX(-11deg)` on the `h1`, never `font-style: italic` (section 10). `background: linear-gradient(180deg, #e8fbff 0%, #9fe3ff 22%, #fff 44%, #1b1140 49%, #2a1a66 51%, #ff4fb0 58%, #ffd3ef 78%, #fff 100%)`, `-webkit-background-clip: text`, `background-clip: text`, `color: transparent`, a white rim from four `drop-shadow(±.006em 0 0 rgba(255,255,255,.75))` filters on each line (amendment WM; was `-webkit-text-stroke: .012em`, which outlined overlapping contours), and on the `h1` `filter: drop-shadow(0 .02em 0 #1b1140) drop-shadow(0 0 .12em rgba(45,226,230,.75)) drop-shadow(0 0 .3em rgba(246,1,157,.55))`. Sizes `min(14vw, 17vh)` and `min(10.5vw, 13vh)`, letter-spacing `.015em` and `.2em` [O: viewed]. At `max-height: 480px`, one line at `min(7.1vw, 17vh)`. Under `forced-colors` the gradient and transparency are dropped.

**Title layout:** a flex column filling the viewport, with light top and bottom scrims only, so the world stays visible. Top block: wordmark, overlapping the top of the disc, which §6.1 keeps at a fixed place, so this composition holds. Bottom block, in order: `THE ROAD NEVER STOPS`, bests, launch button, controls legend. The tagline moved here because over the disc it was hard to read [O: viewed]. The moved position has not been shot. `@media (pointer: coarse)` swaps `PRESS ANY KEY` for `TAP TO LAUNCH`, and the keyboard legend for the touch legend. Until `html[data-mv-boot]` reads `running`, a third span, `LOADING`, replaces either prompt in a dimmed button with no glow (DI-95, VI repair). After 3600 ticks with no pointer or key activity, `data-attract` fades the bottom block except the prompt, so the page serves as a screensaver. Any activity restores it.

**Gesture wiring** per the table in section 4, with the launch button focused at mount; on a coarse pointer with `focusVisible: false`, so the yellow ring does not double the button's edge before anyone asked for focus. It returns once a keyboard moves focus. On leaving DEMO, blur the active element, so Space becomes boost. Chromium already drops focus from the hidden launch button, so that blur matters for a focused element that stays visible, and for engines that keep focus on hidden elements [I]. The title overlay sets `touch-action: none`: with `auto`, a touch drag on it gave `pointerdown` then `pointercancel`, with no `pointerup` or `click`, so nothing launched; with `none` it gave `pointerup` in the `running` state [O: T07/T08 review, Chromium 1234]. `attach(el)` covers only the canvas, and the overlay sits above it.

**Reduced motion:** `data-reduced` on `root` is set at mount from the effective value, then follows `v.reducedMotion`. Under it `ui.css` disables every animation and transition.

**Look criteria, viewed at 1280x720, 390x844 and 3440x1440 (the last scaled to 1920 wide, as in M8):** the chrome has a visible horizon band, no glyph has an unfilled edge, the disc still reads behind it, and the prompt is the obvious next thing. Iterate on `ui.css` until true, and record each pass. **Result:** a title worth looking at. **Proof:** A4, A16 (title images).

### M5. HUD and the cost gates

**Goal:** all readouts, at a measured cost. **Work:** `hud.ts`, HUD parts of `ui.css`.

**Layout is a CSS grid with named areas, not absolute positions,** because the first mock's bar and menu button overlapped on a phone (section 10). The frame sits inside the safe area and, wider than 16:9, inside a central 16:9 box: `left` and `right` are `max(env(safe-area-inset-*, 0px), calc((100vw - 177.78vh) / 2))`.

| Layout | Query | Areas |
|---|---|---|
| Wide | default | `"score . dist"`, `". . boost"`, `". vitals menu"`, rows `auto 1fr auto`, columns `minmax(0, 1fr) minmax(0, 34em) minmax(0, 1fr)` |
| Narrow | `max-aspect-ratio: 1/1` | `"score dist"`, `". ."`, `"menu boost"`, `"vitals vitals"`, rows `auto 1fr auto auto`, two `minmax(0, 1fr)` columns |

`vitals` holds shield pips and the overdrive bar.

**Readouts:**

- Panels: `--mv-panel` fill, 2 px neon edge, soft inset glow. Text gets `0 1px 0 #000, 0 0 3px #000` before its neon glow, so digits stay crisp on anything. Labels are `max(.82em, 10px)`, in `--mv-accent`.
- Score: `min(2.7em, 7.4vw)`, `tabular-nums`, white with accent glow. A `data-long` attribute at 9 or more characters uses `min(2.2em, 6vw)`.
- SYNC: `×8` in `--mv-hot`, about 1.9em, kicked by the beat.
- Shields: one `data-n` attribute on the container, CSS styling skewed pips, filled against outlined, so state does not rely on colour. `aria-label="Shields 2 of 3"`.
- Overdrive: a skewed track with `repeating-linear-gradient` segments, a fill of `linear-gradient(90deg, #2DE2E6, #B026FF 55%, #F6019D 80%, #FFD319)` moved only by `transform: scaleX()`, `role="meter"`, and `aria-valuenow` in 10% steps.
- Distance: km, then sector, then objective, right-aligned, `white-space: nowrap`. If the sector overflows below 360 px wide, drop the palette name there. The width test is part of the sector's gate key and follows the media query's `change` event, so a resize or rotation across 360 px rewrites the sector at once (V1-T08's condition).
- Menu button: `II`, `aria-label="Open cruise menu"`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, and `aria-expanded` written by `menu.ts` when the card opens or closes (VI repair). Under `(pointer: fine)` in PLAY it has `pointer-events: none`, because it sits in the mouse's saturated corner (VI-game); a touch screen keeps it. Boost button: 84 px circle, coarse pointers only, `touch-action: none`, so a thumb that shifts while holding does not end the hold in `pointercancel` [I: same mechanism as the title overlay, not run on this button]. `MUTED` chip beside the menu button.

**Writes:** all text through `Text.data` slots, all style through `style.setProperty` in `slots.ts` or `palette.ts`, attributes through attribute slots, on change only and under the slot throttle (DI-50). Beat: on integer `floor(v.sim.beat)` change, flip `data-beat` between `a` and `b`; two identical keyframe rules restart a compositor-only `transform` animation with no reflow, 1.67 writes per second. Not under reduced motion.

**Result:** a working HUD. **Proof:** A5 to A8, then viewing.

### M6. Cruise menu

**Goal:** a menu that feels like part of the game and works with any device. **Work:** `menu.ts`. `section[role=dialog][aria-modal=true][aria-labelledby]` shown when `mode === CRUISE`. Heading `CRUISE`, then `THE ROAD NEVER STOPS`, then Resume, then music and SFX as native range inputs, mute as a checkbox, quality as a native radio group in `fieldset` and `legend` (Auto, High, Standard, Low, Minimum, Software, with `AUTO (STANDARD)` showing the tier in use), reduced motion as a checkbox, and run stats. Then a one-line note explaining why there is no true pause.

Native controls give arrow-key behaviour and roles for free. Style the range track and thumb with `::-webkit-slider-runnable-track`, `::-webkit-slider-thumb`, `::-moz-range-track`, `::-moz-range-thumb`, because `accent-color` alone left a default white track [O: viewed].

The quality radios are drawn round, with a dot when chosen, and the fieldset has `role="radiogroup"`, so the six read as one choice and not as six checkboxes (VI repair). Focus: on open, focus Resume and mark the HUD `inert`; Resume shows the ring only when a keyboard moved focus last, by `focusVisible` (VI repair). Tab wraps within the dialog. On close, blur to body so Space is boost again. Esc and P close it. The scrim is `rgba(13, 2, 33, .45)`, so the moving world stays visible. The card scrolls internally with `touch-action: pan-y`. At `max-height: 480px` use two columns: heading and Resume on the left, controls on the right, because a single column clipped at 844x390 [O: viewed]. In that layout each stats label sits over its line, centred, and a line may break at its spaces, with a no-break space keeping `KM` by its number: side by side, a 9-digit run reached the MUTE box and THIS RUN crossed the card's padding (V1-T08).

`storage.ts` persists every change. **Result:** the menu works end to end against the recorder. **Proof:** A10, A12, viewing.

### M7. Overlays, popups and fatal

**Goal:** every remaining state in §7.2. **Work:**

- `overlays.ts`. SIGNAL LOST: centred banner, RGB-split `text-shadow`, a stepped flicker that stays under 3 Hz, sub-line `REACQUIRING - SCORE KEPT`. Autopilot tag: `AUTOPILOT - ANY INPUT RETAKES`. Sector banner: on `v.zone.index` change, `SECTOR 08 - ULTRAVIOLET - CRYSTAL DESERT` plus the objective, for 360 ticks. Grade line appended when `Ev.GRADE` arrives. `GRADE` can arrive one frame before `v.zone.index` changes (S-13), so showing the banner must not clear a grade line written the frame before. Context-lost: non-blocking dim layer with `VIDEO SIGNAL INTERRUPTED - THE ROAD IS STILL MOVING`. All state changes write one polite `role="status"` live region, so screen readers get these and not the score.
- `popups.ts`: pool of 6 spans, no node creation after mount, each reused by setting text and flipping between two identical keyframe classes. `transform` and `opacity` only. Each span carries a dark plate, a `::before` in `--mv-panel` with the panels' skew and an edge in the popup's colour, so it stays at 3:1 or more over any backdrop (VI repair, A23). If all 6 are live, the oldest is reused. Under reduced motion a popup has no animation: it appears, and is removed on a `v.tick` expiry, the same timer style as the 360-tick banner (DI-49).
- `fatal.ts` and `copy.ts`: an opaque page with a CSS sunset, `role="alert"`, heading, reason text and a Reload button that receives focus. No animation. The grid's fan lines start at the second row, not at the vanishing point, where 25 lines drew a solid pink wedge (V1-T08). No `perspective` or 3D transform: flat gradients and paths only (§10.1). Must render with `?mount=0`.

| Reason | Heading | Body starts |
|---|---|---|
| `no-webgl2` | NO SIGNAL | This browser or device could not start WebGL2 with floating-point render targets... |
| `context-lost-permanent` | SIGNAL LOST FOR GOOD | The graphics context dropped and did not return within 15 seconds... |
| `shader-error` | BAD TAPE | A shader failed to compile on this GPU... |
| `runtime-error` | THE ROAD BROKE | Something failed while running... |

**Result:** all states exist. **Proof:** A13, viewing.

### M8. Responsive review

**Goal:** it holds at every size, by looking. **Work:** add `geometry:` and `review:` groups. Viewports, each a `test.describe` with `test.use`:

| Name | Size | DPR | Touch |
|---|---|---|---|
| `phone-small` | 320x568 | 2 | `hasTouch`, `isMobile` |
| `phone-portrait` | 390x844 | 3 | same |
| `phone-landscape` | 844x390 | 3 | same |
| `tablet-portrait` | 820x1180 | 2 | same |
| `tablet-landscape` | 1180x820 | 2 | same |
| `laptop` | 1366x768 | 1 | no |
| `desktop` | 1920x1080 | 1 | no |
| `ultrawide` | 3440x1440 | 1 | no |
| `superwide` | 5120x1440 | 1 | no |

States: `title`, `play`, `play-max` (huge score, sync 16, km at chunk 2^44), `play-low` (1 shield, overdrive .1, ghost), `autopilot`, `cruise`, `signal-lost`, `context-lost`, `banner-sector`, `fatal-no-webgl2`.

`review:` freezes CSS animations mid-flight through an injected rule, then saves through `saveShot`. It also builds one contact sheet per state, using `page.setContent` with base64 images on a blank page.

**Viewing list, each opened with the Read tool and described in Progress:**

- Full size, 15 images: `title`, `play`, `cruise` at `phone-portrait`, `phone-landscape`, `tablet-portrait`, `desktop`, `ultrawide`. `ultrawide` is 3440 px wide, over the 1920 px limit in `Agents.md`'s Definition of done, so view those three at 1920 wide through the contact-sheet path.
- Contact sheets, 7 images: `play-max`, `play-low`, `autopilot`, `signal-lost`, `context-lost`, `banner-sector`, `fatal-no-webgl2`.
- Extras, 3 images: `play` on all six palettes at desktop, as one sheet; `play` with `?bg=white`; `title` under `forced-colors`.

**Look criteria:** does it read as a neon arcade HUD and not a web form; can digits be read at a glance at DPR 1; are empty and full pips distinct; is the bar's gradient visible at low fill; are thumbs near boost on a phone; is the ultrawide HUD neither stranded nor tiny; do the six palette tints all look intended; is the sun's column clear in play; does SIGNAL LOST look dramatic rather than broken. Fix in `ui.css`, re-shoot, and record each pass. **Result:** a reviewed layout. **Proof:** A9, A16.

### M9. Accessibility and motion pass

**Goal:** the basics hold in practice. **Work:** add `a11y:`, `contrast:` and `reduced:`. Run the keyboard-only path by hand-written test: Tab, launch, play, Esc, adjust a slider with arrows, change quality with arrows, Esc. **Result:** accessible basics proven. **Proof:** A10 to A12.

### M10. Close

**Goal:** a task the batch can rely on. **Work:** run all of section 7 in one pass. `npm run check:task -- T08`. Paste output, write Outcomes, set `done`. After the batch finishes, the whole-repo `npm run check`. **Result:** ready for T10. **Proof:** A17 to A19.

```powershell
# SUPERSEDED by FH-R2 (4): the loop below stops by command line alone, so it would also stop a Vite server left on these
#   ports by an earlier stage or session, which hazard 1 forbids. Kept as written, not to be run:

#   }
# }
# FH-R2 (4): the same, and never a PID that FH0 recorded. Without FH0's record, nothing is stopped.
$S = 'C:\Users\kgran\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\802ff760ce0bdd56\d050f826-f2f3-4f5f-ab68-28447d8d5879\scratchpad'
$rec = "$S\T08-fs\fh0-listeners.txt"
if (-not (Test-Path $rec)) { "no FH0 record at $($rec): nothing stopped" } else {
  $fh0 = @(Get-Content $rec | Where-Object { $_ -match '^\d+$' } | ForEach-Object { [int]$_ })
  foreach ($port in 5108, 5110, 5118) {
    Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort $port -State Listen -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | ForEach-Object {
      $id = $_.OwningProcess
      $cl = (Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "ProcessId=$id").CommandLine
      if ($fh0 -contains $id) { "left alone, listening at FH0, report it: $port $($id): $cl" }
      elseif ($cl -like "*vite*--port $port*") { Stop-Process -Id $id; "stopped $($id): $cl" }

}
```

A Playwright run stops the dev server it started; this is for one left behind by an interrupted run, or for the 5118 preview at the end of A41. Before stopping one on 5108 or 5110, confirm no run of this task is still using it. **FH-R2 (4):** a listener whose PID FH0 recorded was there before this stage started, so it is left alone and reported in Progress, never stopped, even when its command line is Vite's on that port. If FH0's record is missing, the loop stops nothing and says so. Dry run of FH0's record line and this loop, with `Stop-Process` replaced by a print, in the scratchpad `T08-fs/r2-probe` [O, 2026-11-07]: the record held the PIDs on 5100 and 5173. A Vite-like dummy listener of my own on 5118 was left alone while its PID was in the record, and was marked for stopping once its PID was taken out. With no record, the loop considered nothing. The dummy was then stopped after a check of its command line, and the PIDs on 5100 and 5173 were the same before and after.

## 7. Validation and acceptance

Paste short output into Progress as each passes. In this table, `\|` is a literal pipe, escaped for Markdown. Thresholds marked `[A]` are unmeasured on real UI code. Changing one needs a Decision log entry and the reviewer's agreement. All browser groups are in `tests/visual/ui.spec.ts`, under `gpu`, with T00's auto fixture failing on any console error.

| # | Command or action | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| A0 | `npm run check:task -- T08` at M0 | Exit 1, `VACUOUS` |

**Human checks.** They do not block `done`. Outcomes reports them as not verified, and T11's operator pack collects them (`Agents.md`).

- **HC1.** Layout, touch and targets on real phones and tablets, since touch and metrics are only emulated here.
- **HC2.** Whether iOS Safari's activation starts audio at launch.
- **HC3.** Android and iOS font fallback for the wordmark and HUD (§7.5, DI-30).
- **HC4.** Pen input.
- **HC5.** Whether the controls feel good (§14 risk 3).
- **HC6.** UI frame cost on a phone.
- **HC7.** §14 risk 8, which remains the operator's decision.

### Amendment FH, validation and acceptance

All browser rows are in `tests/visual/ui.spec.ts` on `gpu`, port 5108, through the GPU lock, under T00's auto fixture, which fails a test on any console error. "Fail-before" is the run of FH1 on the unchanged source; a guard passes both before and after, and says so. No existing threshold moves. The four expected-output edits, E1 to E4, are in section 11 and need the reviewer's agreement under `Agents.md`. In this table `\|` is a literal pipe.

| A30 | `-g "fullscreen:"`, ~~11 tests on the dev page: 9 desktop~~ ~~**FH-R1: 12 tests on the dev page: 10 desktop**~~ **FH-V1: 13 tests on the dev page: 11 desktop**, then a tap row at 390x844 (DPR 3) and at 1024x768 (DPR 2), both `hasTouch`, `isMobile` | **FH-R1 (2), the event counter, for every case below:** before any page script, an init script counts `fullscreenchange`, `webkitfullscreenchange`, `fullscreenerror` and `webkitfullscreenerror` on `document` and logs `MV_FS <type> <n>` with `console.log` for each. ~~Registered before the product's listeners, it runs first in the same dispatch, so once the count has moved the product has handled that event too.~~ **FH-R2 (3):** Once the count has moved, the product has handled that event too. Listener order does not give that. It holds because the dispatch is synchronous, so no `waitForFunction` poll, `evaluate` or console-driven read can run between the counter's listener and the product's [I: from the DOM's synchronous dispatch, not probed]. ~~Each case reads the count before its action, then waits for it to move before it calls `step()` or reads the box or `fullscreenElement`.~~ **FH-R2 (3):** The wait applies only to an action expected to fire one of the four events. That means an action that changes fullscreen state (a click, tap or Space on the box, a page-script exit, the untick), and (5)'s refused request, which fires `fullscreenerror`. ~~Before such an action the case reads the count, and it waits for the count to move before it calls `step()` or reads the box or `fullscreenElement`.~~ **FH-R3 (1) (iv):** Below, such an action is a *fullscreen action*. Before a fullscreen action the case reads the count, its 'before' count, except in (5) (next), and it waits for the count to move before it calls `step()` or reads the box or `fullscreenElement`. Every 'before' count, for a fullscreen action or for one of the actions below that must leave fullscreen unchanged, is taken only after the previous fullscreen action's wait has finished, so it already includes that action's event. **FH-R3 (2):** (5)'s 'before' count is 0 by construction. It is taken from the console lines the test collects, which hold no `MV_FS` line before the timer fires, and is not read from the page. The reason: `page.evaluate` and `waitForFunction` grant activation [O: `Agents.md`, T10 rehearsal], and a page-side read before the 1 s timer might leave activation in place when the timer's request runs, so the request might succeed on correct code [I; how long Chromium keeps activation is from memory, M]. (5) then waits only on `page.waitForEvent('console')` for `MV_FS fullscreenerror 1`, as below. An action that must leave fullscreen unchanged gets no wait, because the count would never move and the wait would time out on correct code. ~~Those actions are Escape in CRUISE and in PLAY while fullscreen, P then P, Resume (in (8) and (12)), and (9)'s `setViewportSize`.~~ **FH-R3 (1) (i), (ii):** Those actions are taken in fullscreen once it has settled: Escape in CRUISE and in PLAY, P then P, the Resume after Escape in (8), (12)'s Resume, which comes after the re-entry's count has moved, and (9)'s `setViewportSize`. The Resume straight after the untick in (8)'s last case is not one of them. It falls inside the untick's own exit, so it comes under the untick's wait: once the count has moved, and after the `step(2)` that follows, the case reads the count up by one from its value before the untick and `fullscreenElement` `null`. ~~For each, after the action and after the `step()` that follows it where the case has one, the row reads the count unchanged from its value before the action and `fullscreenElement` still `<html>`, beside the case's own reads.~~ **FH-R3 (1) (iii):** For each of those actions, the row reads the count unchanged from its 'before' value and `fullscreenElement` still `<html>`, beside the case's own reads. It reads them right after the action, and again after a `step()` only when that step comes before the case's next fullscreen action. The evidence that the count stays put: after a synthetic Escape in fullscreen, P1 logged none of the four events within 600 ms in any of its three runs [O: P1, `probe-fs.log` lines 7, 15, 23]. After `setViewportSize`, P3 logged a `resize` and no `fullscreenchange` in each of its three runs [O: P3, `probe-app.log`]. P3 listened only for `fullscreenchange`, which was the only change event Chromium 1234 fired on a standard-API transition in P1 [O: P1]. P and Resume call no fullscreen function in this design [I]. A case with no fullscreen action, such as (7), reads no count. Case (5) must grant no activation, so it waits with `page.waitForEvent('console')` for its `MV_FS fullscreenerror 1` line, which grants none [O: `Agents.md`, T10 rehearsal]. Every other case uses `waitForFunction` on the count. The reason: an exit settles its promise with `fullscreenElement` already `null` before the event fires (FH-27). (1) FULLSCREEN is shown (headless reports `fullscreenEnabled` true [O]), unticked, first in `.mv-toggles`; Tab from RESUME visits music, sfx, mute-toggle, the checked radio, fullscreen, hide-hud, reduced, then resume, so the trap's ends are unchanged. (2) A mouse click on its label: `document.fullscreenElement` is `<html>`, ticked, no `togglePause`; a second click: `null`, unticked, still `cruise`. (3) Tab to it, Space: in and ticked; Space: out and unticked. (4) **Mirror:** in fullscreen, a page-script `document.exitFullscreen()`, standing in for Esc, the browser's control or the OS (FH-19), unticks the box after `fullscreenchange`, with no `togglePause` in CRUISE. (5) **Refusal:** an init script ticks the box and dispatches `change` from a timer 1 s after load, and the test makes no `evaluate`, locator call or input before reading, so there is no activation: the request is refused (`TypeError: Permissions check failed` and `fullscreenerror` here [O]), the box ends unticked, `fullscreenElement` is `null`, and no console error (an unhandled rejection would be one). **FH-R1 (7), how the console is read:** T00's fixture stays on (`allowConsoleErrors` false), so any `console.error` and any `pageerror` fail the row. Chromium 1234 reports this refusal as a `warning`, not an error: `Failed to execute 'requestFullscreen' on 'Element': API can only be initiated by a user gesture.` [O: P5 B]. The test also collects console messages itself and requires one `warning` with exactly that text, so a run in which no request was made cannot pass. An unhandled refusal arrives as `pageerror: TypeError: Permissions check failed` [O: P5 B], which the fixture fails, so f7 still has a catcher. If a later Chromium logs the refusal as an error, the fixture fails this row and names the text. The row would then move to `allowConsoleErrors: true` with its own check that allows only that exact text and still fails on any `pageerror`. That change goes in the Decision log when needed, and is not made ahead of time. (6) **Prefixed only:** an init script removes `requestFullscreen`, `exitFullscreen`, `fullscreenEnabled` and `fullscreenElement` from the prototypes; the row shows; a click: `webkitFullscreenElement` is `<html>`, ticked; a page-script `webkitExitFullscreen()`: unticked [O: P1, Chromium then fires only `webkitfullscreenchange`]. (7) **Unavailable:** both APIs removed, the iPhone stand-in: no FULLSCREEN label rendered, ~~`getByLabel('FULLSCREEN')` count 0~~ **FH-R1 (1):** the label's box 0x0 with computed `display: none`, and `getByRole('checkbox', { name: 'FULLSCREEN' })` `toHaveCount(0)`. `getByLabel` would count 1 on correct code, because Playwright 1.64.1's label engine keeps a hidden element: on P6's page, with the label not rendered, `getByLabel` counted 1 while `getByRole` counted 0, `.filter({ visible: true })` 0, and `isHidden()` was true [O: P6]. Tab visits music, sfx, mute-toggle, radio, hide-hud, reduced, resume; ~~the same with `fullscreenEnabled` false and the functions present.~~ **FH-R2 (2):** the same with both `fullscreenEnabled` and `webkitFullscreenEnabled` reading false, and all four request and exit functions present. That is the iframe-without-`allow` case FH-5 names, where both flags are false [M]. With only the standard flag false, correct code takes the prefixed pair under FH-5 and shows the row, because Chromium 1234 also reports `webkitFullscreenEnabled` true and has `webkitRequestFullscreen` and `webkitExitFullscreen` [O: P1, `probe-fs.log` lines 5, 13, 21]. (8) **Esc and leaving:** in CRUISE in fullscreen, Escape: no `togglePause`, still fullscreen and `cruise`; Resume: `play`, still fullscreen; Escape in PLAY: no `togglePause`; P, then P: `cruise`, then `play`; in PLAY, a page-script exit, then `step(2)`: exactly one more `togglePause`, `cruise`, unticked, RESUME focused; in CRUISE, a page-script exit: no `togglePause`; in `signal-lost`, a page-script exit, then frames back into PLAY: no `togglePause`; untick in CRUISE, Resume at once, wait for `fullscreenElement` `null`, `step(2)`: no `togglePause` beyond Resume's. **FH-R1 (2):** every page-script exit above, and the untick, is followed by the counter wait before `step()`, so "in PLAY, a page-script exit, then `step(2)`" reads "a page-script exit, the count moves, `step(2)`". In the last case, ~~wait for `fullscreenElement` `null`~~ becomes "the count moves". Under f3 the change event then always sets the leave flag before the step, so the extra `togglePause` shows however the event and the Resume click are timed. (9) **Fill:** in fullscreen the stage and `.mv-ui` equal the viewport, and after `setViewportSize(1280, 720)` it is still fullscreen and both read 1280x720 [O: P3]. **FH-R1 (8), what (9) shows:** headless keeps the viewport size in fullscreen [O: P1, P3], so "equal the viewport" holds whether or not the page is fullscreen. (9) shows only that `fullscreenElement` is `<html>` and that the layout follows `setViewportSize` while fullscreen stays on. Fill on a real screen is HC8, HC9 and HC11. (10, 11) A tap on the label enters and ticks, a second tap leaves and unticks, at each touch size. **FH-R1 (6),** (12) **Unticked in the gap** (FH-26), desktop: in fullscreen in CRUISE, a page script calls `document.exitFullscreen()` and, in its `then`, clicks the box by script. That is the moment after an exit by the browser and before its change event, with `fullscreenElement` already `null` [O: P5 D, 3 of 3]. After the count moves: no `pageerror`, no console error, the box unticked, `fullscreenElement` `null`; `step(2)`: no `togglePause`, still `cruise`. Then a click enters again, Resume, a page-script exit in PLAY, the count moves, `step(2)`: exactly one `togglePause` and `cruise`, so no own-exit mark was left behind. Without FH-26 the product's own `exitFullscreen()` there rejects with `TypeError: Failed to execute 'exitFullscreen' on 'Document': Document not active`, and unhandled it arrives as a `pageerror` [O: P5 C, D]. **FH-V1,** (13) **The first frame after a leave in PLAY** (FH-36, FH-37), desktop: in fullscreen, `state('play')` (PLAY, autopilot off, `hud`), read under the no-change rule; a page-script exit, the count moves; then one `step()` and, in the same task, the read: `data-view` `hud menu`, `data-mv-mode` `cruise`, the live region as before the exit (not `AUTOPILOT - ANY INPUT RETAKES`), RESUME focused, one more `togglePause`. The next frame reads the same. **Human checks for FH.** They do not block `done`. Each needs the operator's `dist/` rebuilt first, which this task may not do (section 13, FH). T11's operator pack collects them.

- **HC8.** FULLSCREEN on the operator's phone: ticked in CRUISE, the road, HUD and card fill the screen with nothing under a notch or a system bar; leaving by the system gesture or back unticks the box, and from PLAY leaves the run in CRUISE; rotating in fullscreen keeps it filled. Over Tailscale the page is plain HTTP; the Fullscreen API is not limited to secure contexts [M], and if the browser refuses there, that is the finding.
- **HC9.** FULLSCREEN on the operator's tablet, and on an iPad in Safari if that is the tablet: the row is there, ticking enters fullscreen, Safari's own close control and swipe leave it and untick the box, and they cover nothing needed, II included. **FH-R1 (8):** the road, HUD and card fill the tablet's screen in fullscreen, in both orientations, with no band left over. No browser row can show fill, because headless keeps the viewport size.
- **HC10.** Safari on an iPhone: no FULLSCREEN row; HIDE HUD and REDUCED MOTION share the row; nothing else in the card changed.
- **HC11.** Esc in fullscreen on a real desktop browser (Chrome or Edge, and Firefox; Safari if at hand): from PLAY one Esc leaves fullscreen, unticks the box and leaves the game in CRUISE; from CRUISE one Esc leaves fullscreen and the menu stays open; P toggles as usual in fullscreen; the browser's fullscreen notice hides nothing needed. **FH-R1 (8):** in fullscreen the road, HUD and card fill the monitor, with no window-sized frame left in the middle.
- **HC12.** HIDE HUD on the operator's phone and tablet: in PLAY only II is left, a second finger still boosts, a tap on II opens the menu, and the setting survives a reload. And whether the autopilot tag should stay with the HUD hidden (section 11 keeps it).

## 8. Idempotence and recovery

- All test and check commands are safe to re-run. Browser tests use fresh contexts, so `localStorage` starts empty and nothing persists between runs.
- Clean state for this task: `Remove-Item -Recurse -Force test-results\T08, test-results\T08-review -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue`.
- `dev/ui.html` holds only a backdrop and five lines of script, and can be rebuilt from M2.
- No git here. Before a large restyle, copy `src/ui/ui.css` outside the project. Every look iteration is one file.
- Never run `npm install`, change `package.json`, edit `tests/support/**`, or run whole-repo `npm run check` while siblings are mid-flight.
- If a sibling's half-written file breaks a run, `check:task -- T08` should still pass; if it does not, that is a T00 defect, recorded and escalated, not worked around.

**Amendment FH:**

- Before the first FH edit, the files FH touches are copied by bytes, with a sha256 list, to the scratchpad `T08-fs/backup` (section 6). To undo FH, copy them back and check the hashes; `src/ui/fullscreen.ts` and `tests/unit/ui/fullscreen.test.ts` are new and are deleted. The plan before this amendment is at the scratchpad `T08-fs/plan-backup/T08-ui-input.md.before-FH`, sha256 prefix `f40f92976d54`. **FH-R1:** the plan as the first review read it is at `T08-fs/plan-backup/T08-ui-input.md.before-FH-R1`, sha256 prefix `ab44664fbd75`. The probes P5 to P7 live in `T08-fs/probe` beside P1 to P4, with their logs, and can be rerun against a dev server of their own on 5128.
- Every FH run is safe to repeat. Browser contexts start with empty storage, and seeded settings come from each test's own init script. Fullscreen is per page and ends with the page.
- The scratch build folder `T08-fs/dist` and `test-results/T08/fh*` may be deleted at any time; they are not `dist/` and nothing reads them.
- The 5118 preview and any dev server this task starts are stopped by the command-line check in section 6. A server whose command line does not show this task's port is left alone, and so is everything on 5100 and 5173.

SIGNAL LOST dramatic: met. One reviewer, on a CSS backdrop, stills plus two motion sheets. Whether any of this holds over the real scene is T10's and T11's to see.

**Look passes, each after viewing:**

1. A `.mv-ui button` reset outranked every button's own class, so Launch and Resume had no fill or border, and both legends showed at once. Reset moved into `:where()`, and the coarse-pointer rules raised.
2. `phone-small` radios collided, `AUTO (STANDARD)` over `HIGH`: two columns under 480 px wide.
3. SYNC had no label at wide sizes, which the aria snapshot exposed: each label now sits over its value by `display: contents`.
4. Phone-landscape: one-line wordmark, columns that fit `play-max`'s sector, banner and tag below the panels, HUD hidden behind the card at 600 px tall or less, note brought back.
5. Popups larger, and white for gains so they survive the bright horizon. SIGNAL LOST's sub-line scales with its heading. Banner and context-lost text balanced. `2ND FINGER / BOOST BOOST` corrected to `2ND FINGER / BUTTON BOOST`. Fatal card less see-through.
6. Pip and panel inset glows reduced for contrast, section 10.

### FC repair, A10's negative control (VI-gates)

VI-gates: A10's control was a bare `test.fail`, which Playwright counts as expected whenever the test fails at all, so it could not tell the wrap check failing from a broken fixture. Port 5108, output `test-results/T08-FC/<run>`. Backups and sha256 of the spec and this plan, the mutation script `mutate.mjs` and its saved originals in the session scratchpad `T08/FC`. Every browser run went through `gpu-lock.mjs`, one command per call. Each broken fixture was applied by `mutate.mjs` and restored by bytes, with `restored true` every time.

- [x] 2026-11-06T12:13Z Status set to `in-progress`, with a section 14 note. Backups taken at 12:11:55Z: spec `8cbba871b4ec`, plan `da2a9aa35eff`.
- [x] 2026-11-06T12:16Z **Fail-before, on the unchanged spec,** `-g "control: with Tab blocked"`, 12:13:50Z to 12:16:12Z. The reasons come from each run's `error-context.md`:

  | Run | Fixture | Exit | Reported | What the failure actually was |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | o0 | clean | 0 | `1 passed (3.6s)`, marked ✘ | `Expected: "resume"`, `Received: "outside"`: the wrap check |
  | o1 | page never ready: the dev page's module answered by a stub that installs nothing | **1** | `1 failed`, 1.0m | `Test timeout of 60000ms exceeded`, at `locator.focus`, waiting for `[data-mv="reduced"]` |
  | o1b | never ready, with a 5 s `waitForFunction` for `window.__ui` in the fixture | **0** | `1 passed (8.9s)` | `TimeoutError: page.waitForFunction: Timeout 5000ms exceeded` |
  | o2 | ready in the wrong state, `play` for `cruise` | **0** | `1 passed (3.8s)` | `Received: "menu-button"`: no dialog at all |
  | o3 | the outside-buttons fixture throws | **0** | `1 passed (3.8s)` | `SyntaxError: Failed to execute 'insertAdjacentHTML'` |

  So the old control took a bounded-wait timeout, a wrong state and a thrown fixture as its proof. It did not take a test-level timeout: Playwright 1.64.1 records that as `timedOut`, not the `failed` that `test.fail` expects (section 10).
- [x] 2026-11-06T12:17Z **Change,** in `tests/visual/ui.spec.ts` only. The row and its control share one check, `expectTabWraps`: focus `reduced`, assert focus is there, press Tab, then assert the wrap, `toBe('resume')` under the message `Tab from the last control in the dialog lands on the first`. The outside buttons are named apart, `outside-start` and `outside-end`. The control is now a plain `test`. It asserts the fixture first: `window.__ui` installed and `data-mv-mode` `cruise`, the dialog exposed by role, the buttons before and after `#ui`. Then it catches the shared check's failure and requires `{ stopped: 1, check: <the wrap message>, matcher: 'toBe', expected: 'resume', received: 'outside-end' }` from the listener's count and the caught error's `matcherResult`. A non-soft `expect` that is caught does not fail a test in 1.64.1: it throws, and only a soft error calls `_failWithError` (`lib/matchers/expect.js`, `lib/worker/workerProcessEntry.js`). Spec sha256 after the change `3ea878f3c2ce`.
- [x] 2026-11-06T12:17Z **Pass-after,** `-g "a11y:"`, 12:17:01Z: **exit 0, `15 passed (8.5s)`**, no ✘ in the list. The control printed `A10 control: {"stopped":1,"check":"Tab from the last control in the dialog lands on the first","matcher":"toBe","expected":"resume","received":"outside-end"}`.
- [x] 2026-11-06T12:19Z **The same broken fixtures on the new control,** 12:17:22Z to 12:19:01Z, each restored to `3ea878f3c2ce`, and `sha256sum -c` OK after the last one. Every one is now a failure, each with its own reason:

  | Run | Fixture | Exit | Reported |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | n1 | page never ready, as o1 | **1** | `1 failed` in 327 ms: `the dev page is ready, in the cruise state`, received `installed: false, mode: null` |
  | n1b | never ready with the 5 s wait, as o1b | **1** | `1 failed`: `TimeoutError: page.waitForFunction: Timeout 5000ms exceeded`, which o1b had counted as passed |
  | n2 | wrong state, as o2 | **1** | `1 failed`: received `mode: "play"` where `cruise` is wanted |
  | n3 | outside-buttons fixture throws, as o3 | **1** | `1 failed`: `SyntaxError: Failed to execute 'insertAdjacentHTML'`, at `withOutsideButtons` |
  | n4 | the listener counts the Tab but no longer stops it, so the trap works and the wrap check passes | **1** | `1 failed`: `with Tab blocked, the wrap check must fail`, `Received: null`; log `{"stopped":1,"check":"null"}` |
  | n5 | `reduced` disabled, so the check fails at its first assertion, not the wrap | **1** | `1 failed`: received `check: "focus is on the last control before the Tab"`, `expected: "reduced"`, `received: "resume"`, `stopped: 0` |

  n4 is the direction `test.fail` also caught, as an unexpected pass. n5 shows that a failed `expect` inside the same check does not count unless it is the wrap assertion.
