## 10. Surprises and discoveries

All from 2026-11-05 scratchpad rehearsals on Chromium 1234. None has run against project code.

**Activation.** Fresh page per case, real input, `new AudioContext().state` inside the handler:

| Event | Input | State |
|---|---|---|
| `pointerdown`, `pointerup`, `click` | Mouse | `running` |
| `keydown` | `a`, Tab | `running` |
| `keydown` | Escape | `suspended` |
| `keydown` activation only, no context made | Shift, Control | `isActive` false |
| `pointerdown` | Touch tap | `suspended` |
| `pointerup`, `click` | Touch tap | `running` |
| `pointerup` | Touch drag, 8 moves | `running` |
| `click` | Touch drag | Never fires |

That rehearsal page had `touch-action: none` on `body`, so the drag rows hold only under that rule (M4). So launch must never hang on touch `pointerdown` alone, and `click` alone would miss a drag. iOS Safari was not tested; whether it treats `pointerup` as a gesture is unknown [M], which is why `click` also calls `launch`.

- **My first version of that test was vacuous.** A `page.evaluate` that installed the listener granted activation, and every case read `running`, including touch `pointerdown`. Moving the listener into the page's own script gave the table above. This is the trap `Agents.md` describes, and it means no polling wait may precede the first gesture.
- **`MutationObserver` misses same-value style writes.** Twice-repeated same-value writes gave: `text.data` 2 records, `className` 2, `dataset` 2, `textContent` 2, `style.transform` 0, `style.setProperty` 0, custom property 0. A counter built on the observer alone would pass a per-frame redundant bar write.
- **A heap-usage delta does not detect short-lived allocation.** With `Runtime.getHeapUsage` around 100k calls, a function allocating a 2-element array each call read 0.09 bytes per call, against 0.11 to 0.15 for a clean function. The sampling profiler with both `includeObjectsCollected*` flags separated them: clean 3,820 to 5,832 bytes total, array 4,029,040, template string 9,820,820, attributed by function name. The first run of any function shows 223 KB from warm-up. This may help T09 with §14 risk 6.
- **Bar movement causes no layout; text does.** Over 2 s of real rAF on the mock: transform writes gave `LayoutCount` 0 and 331 style recalcs at 11.07 ms total; text writes gave 323 layouts at 33.47 ms total, with no containment applied; nothing gave 0 and 0. rAF ran at about 165 Hz headless, so the gates use per-frame ratios and simulated seconds rather than wall-clock rates.
- **`background-clip: text` with `font-style: italic` leaves part of a glyph unfilled.** The slanted T overhung its box, and its arm showed only the stroke, in both Impact and Arial Black. Upright glyphs under a `skewX` transform on the parent fixed it [O: viewed].
- **Three wordmark faces viewed at 1280x720.** Courier New bold reads as a typewriter, with strokes too thin to carry the chrome band. Impact and Arial Black both carry it. Arial Black was the strongest. One reviewer, one palette, a CSS sun.
- **The mock's absolute layout collided on a phone.** At 390x844, the bar was `16,816 to 374,829` and the menu button `333,787 to 377,831`. Viewing caught it, and the rect dump confirmed it. Hence the grid.
- **Contrast over a white backdrop.** Measured with hidden text and 95th-percentile background: a panel gradient of .62 to .42 alpha gave `.sync` 1.03, labels 3.14, values 2.4 to 4.1. A flat .8 with `#FF3FB4` gave `.sync` 3.56, all else above 7. A flat .8 with `#FF7FD0` (luminance about .41) gave 4.94. On the scene backdrop every readout exceeded 5.9.
- **The menu clipped at 844x390** with one column, cutting off the motion row.
- **`?inline` CSS through Vite 8 build:** no CSS asset, `-webkit-text-stroke`, both `background-clip` forms [I: two matches], `env(safe-area-inset-left)` and `100dvh` all survived minification.
- **Two A9 first-run failures are already known from these starting values** [O: T07/T08 review, M4's CSS in Chromium 1234, Arial Black installed]. At `phone-landscape` the wordmark is 410 px, 48.6% of the viewport, under A9's 55%; the other phones read 86.5%. There `play-max`'s `S175921860444 ARCTIC LASER` is 172 px at 11 px and 156 px at 10 px, in a side column of 160 px before its border and padding, and M5's drop rule stops at 360 px wide. Both are `ui.css` work in M8, and A9's thresholds stand.
- **Installed on this machine, relevant to font fallback:** Arial Black, Impact, Segoe UI Black, Courier New, Consolas, Bahnschrift. Phone font stacks were not available to test.

**Found while building, on project code, 2026-11-05.**

- **The two predicted A9 failures arrived as predicted.** Wordmark 49.4% at `phone-landscape`, and the `play-max` sector leaving its panel there and at `phone-small`: `clip: sector {"x":128.57...,"w":171.64} leaves its panel {"x":163.84...,"w":146.26}`. No threshold moved.
- **A fractional double passed between closures allocates, and the gate saw it.** A7's first run: 2,473,624 B unchanged, 24 B per pair. Two numbers crossed a call each frame: `runU` and `runU / 1000`. Moving them into a `Float64Array(1)` and keeping gate keys as small integers gave 44,296 B, with no other change. `allocProbe` at 3.2 MB shows the gate had been working. This is `Agents.md`'s boxed-double note at work in a place nobody expected it.
- **Most of A7's remaining total is the first evaluate, not the UI.** A sampled empty loop in the first evaluate of a session read 164,680 B, and a second and third sampled pass of the pairs read 10,008 B and 3,852 B, the mock's level. A gate that passes on the first sampled pass is the conservative reading, so it stays that way.
- **`formatKm` could print a floor that rounds down too far.** `floor(1.001 * 1000)` is 1000 in binary, so 1.001e6 km printed `1.000E+6`. The `kmKey` test caught it. Digits now come from one integer division.
- **The first same-value `setProperty` can record once.** A5n's control read `styleRecords: 1`, then 0 after one priming write, presumably as the attribute re-serialises [I]. The plan's "0 records" holds from the second write on, and the control primes.
- **A page does one layout of its own 300 to 600 ms after load, with no UI frame running.** Windows of 300 ms on a page doing nothing: delta 0, then 1, then 0 for every overdrive window after. Cause not established. A6 charged it to the bar until the test waited for a quiet spell. Only the wait changed, and the expected 0 did not.
- **Chromium delivers `pointermove` aligned to frames.** A CDP drag of 60 px read .4167 rather than .5 when sampled at once: the last 10 px was queued. Waiting two rAFs gave exactly .5. Input code unchanged.
- **Vite's file watcher can fire 10 s or more after an edit,** and a page connected at that moment reloads. `vite.log` shows `page reload src/ui/index.ts` at 7:36:34 local, and the first test of the run started just then failed with `Execution context was destroyed`. A second symptom was a live page reading `demo` for 5 s after its launch. Both went away with no change, and each followed an edit. So a final pass must start well after the last edit, and this one did: no reload in the log during it.
- **With nothing else focusable, headless Chromium wraps Tab by itself.** A10's `test.fail` control first passed with the trap blocked, so it was not evidence. It now injects a button before and after the UI, and then the blocked trap lets focus out.
- **The forced-colours backplate cut `MIDNIGHT` off at the feet.** Text in forced colours gets a backplate, and `VECTOR`'s painted over the line above it. Seen only by opening the image. `forced-color-adjust: none` on the two lines, with `CanvasText`.
- **Glow counts against contrast.** Labels read 4.48 over white on palettes 3 and 5, against 5.06 by arithmetic for the flat panel. The panel's inset glow and the filled pips' glow were lifting the 95th percentile behind the label. Both reduced, and the lowest is now 5.06. Threshold unchanged.
- **A tap can reach the HUD underneath.** If the HUD replaces the title between `pointerup` and the following `click`, the click lands on whatever is now under the finger. Forced in a test: with the guard off, `togglePause` was recorded on 3 of 3 runs; with it on, none. Whether a real device hits that window was not observed.
- **A5's cruise estimate of 17 was low.** It measured 23 at first, because 8% overdrive steps cross a 10% `aria-valuenow` boundary on most shards. A 60-tick throttle on that one attribute gave 19.2. This test also wraps overdrive rather than letting it fill and stop, which is worse than real play.
- **The Bash tool failed on a heredoc containing apostrophes,** as warned. Scripts went through the Write tool.

**Found in the R1 repair, 2026-11-05.**

- **A bordered readout measured itself.** A11 took the 95th-percentile luminance of the whole box. The MUTED chip is the one readout with a border, 2 px in `--mv-hot`, lifted to luminance .40 or more, so the 95th percentile was the border: white text read 1.33 to 2.31 against its own edge, and a dimmed chip still "passed" the dimmed control. Measuring inside border and padding fixed both; the log shows the chip is the only readout this changes, and every earlier number is identical (5.06 lowest over white, 8.45 over the scene).
- **Plain `cruise` clipped too, not only long runs.** At 740x360 THIS RUN sat 8 px into the card's left padding with an 8-digit score, because the two-item row was wider than the 15em column and the column centred it.
- **`focusVisible: false` works in Chromium 1234.** The coarse launch button had focus and no `:focus-visible`, and Shift+Tab, Tab brought the ring back. Other engines were not tried; one that ignores the option shows the ring as before.
- **The stacked card line orphaned `KM`.** A 25-character line wrapped before `KM`, seen only in the image. A no-break space before it moves the break to the slash. The aria snapshot normalises it, and still matches.
- **A dev state that goes from DEMO straight to CRUISE now shows 0 km,** which is correct under DI-61. So `state()` runs the launch and the run so far in scoring PLAY, then switches to the state's own mode. Every state's km is what it was before the repair, for example `cruise` still `12.3`.
- **The Resume button shows the ring when the menu opens in the dev page on touch.** The page drives the mode by script, with no pointer event first. After a real tap on `II`, Chromium's heuristic would probably not show it [I: not run]. Left as it is.

**Found in the R2 repair, 2026-11-05.**

- **V2's inferred cause holds.** Moving only the pixel loop into the page, with the same screenshot, took each A13 reason test from 32 s to under 1 s on the same machine [O: 21:53Z and 22:00Z runs]. So about 31 s of each was returning 8.3M numbers through `evaluate`, not the screenshot. A11's `decode` still returns channels, but over small readout clips; `contrast:` tests were not slow and were left alone.

**Found in the D4 repair, 2026-11-06.** Chromium 1234 headless, D3D11, one RTX 4070 SUPER, fresh browsers.

- **Opacity 0 and `visibility: hidden` draw nothing ahead.** The HUD laid out in DEMO at either kept both long frames (27.0 to 32.2 ms), with or without `will-change` [O: probe]. Opacity .001 or .01 removes the first; the frame at +1.6 s goes only when the HUD also has a layer of its own. That the compositor skips a fully transparent layer is from memory of Chromium's design [M]; the measurement is what stands.
- **The +1.6 s frame has no DOM change behind it,** in four runs, and `will-change: opacity` alone, with no warm-up, keeps it. What runs there is not established. One reading, untested, is a re-raster of the HUD after its opacity settles, with text drawn another way [I].
- **The cost is per browser process, not per draw.** The menu warmed in DEMO only, then `display: none` all through PLAY, still opened in 6.3 to 13.5 ms where the unwarmed one took 28 to 36. So whatever is paid once, plausibly shader compiles in a GPU process with an empty cache [I: consistent with "fresh browser only" and with the main thread idle, not shown], was paid during DEMO.
- **So a control injected late is not a control.** `addStyleTag` after `MV_READY`, restoring the old path, read the same as the fix, because the warm panels had already been drawn. A20's control adopts its sheet in an init script before any element exists, and reads 58.5 to 72.9 ms.
- **The dev page shows the HUD's cost more strongly than the app:** 52 to 73 ms against the app's 26 to 36, with no WebGL running. The menu's first open is weaker there (20.5 to 36.4) and is printed, not gated.
- **Playwright counts opacity .001 as visible.** In DEMO `toBeVisible()` now passes for `[data-mv="hud"]` and `[data-mv="cruise"]`. No spec of T08, T09 or T10 asserts them hidden in DEMO [grep, and the 21 sibling rows passed], but any later one must read `data-view`, `aria-hidden` or opacity.
- **A9 now also measures the warm panels in the title state,** since its `shown()` reads box and visibility. That only adds checks, and all 111 passed, so `shown()` was left alone.

**Found in the VI repair, 2026-11-06.** Chromium 1234 headless, D3D11, one RTX 4070 SUPER; dev server on port 5108.

- **After the hand-back, the next Tab starts from II's place.** II is the last control a keyboard can reach in PLAY, so that Tab left the page (`activeElement` body) and a second Tab reached II [O: printed by A21, "Tab once body, twice menu-button"]. A21's first form assumed one Tab and failed on correct code; the Enter case now starts from a fresh page.
- **A mutation survived the first A24 tap case.** On touch the modality starts as "pointer", so a tap that failed to clear keyboard modality (m5) still showed no ring: 3 passed under m5. A keyboard now opens and closes the card first, and m5 then fails with `visible: true`.
- **The hand-back hides a button that keeps every key.** With m1, a focused II in PLAY still steered, because the blur runs first. Only RESUME in CRUISE, which the hand-back does not cover, shows m1 in the browser; the unit test shows it too.
- **The dev server mounts the title late.** A cold load showed the title 367 to 571 ms after commit in six runs, and `running` at 2.1 to 2.4 s. So A26 cannot press at 200 ms and still meet a listener; it presses at mount, 371 to 576 ms in. On the old code that press built an `AudioContext` `running` 68 ms later and the run started under the title (fail-before, 10:40Z).
- **Forced together, the right-hand popups overlap.** A23 shows all six pool spans at once; at 1920x1080 `+1200` (58%), `GRAZE +100` (66%) and `HIT` (74%) overlap, and with plates a later popup now hides part of an earlier one where before their glyphs crossed [O: viewed]. In play, starts are 100 ms apart and spans alternate sides, so neighbours are two starts apart and both live only under a burst of events within .9 s; how often that happens was not measured.
- **Old popups over the real scene,** fail-before, 4 held frames: lowest p10 HIT 1.19, MISS 1.38, GRAZE 1.66, `+1200` 2.07, `GRADE A` 2.45, `+500` 3.32, as VI-game measured (HIT 1.18 to 1.68). Over white the old style read 1.00 for the white popups and HIT exactly 3.00.
- **T10's A18n floor clip includes the popup band.** It takes two page screenshots of y 62% to 82% 600 and 850 ms after stopping the loop, and popups sit at 64% and animate on CSS for .9 s whatever the loop does. A popup started right at the stop already broke it on the old CSS (716 pixels at a 0 ms stop). The plate's outer glow carried a rising popup below its own box for another 200 ms, and A18's fixed schedule landed there 3 times of 3. Without the glow the window is the old one, and A18 passed 2 of 2 and in the final sibling run. The dependence is still there and is T10's (section 13).
- **Sibling rows moved under me.** In one sibling run A17b's boot faults took `main.ts`'s `BOOT_FAILED` path instead of T10's fatal route, A16d's buffer did not follow a DPR change, and the hitch control's second page never received its click; in the next run on the same T08 code all three passed, as on the pre-repair source. `boot.ts` still rethrows in its outer catch (VI-r1's blocking finding, T10's), so where A17b's fault lands decides that row. Other owners edited `src/sim`, `src/render/terrain`, `src/harness` and T10's e2e specs during this session.
- **My first mutation script assumed CRLF.** A whole-file check with `grep -c $'\r$'` in Git Bash counted every line, but a byte count shows every owned file is LF, as before this repair; m2 then failed to apply and its first run was on clean code, recorded as such and rerun.

**Found in the FC repair, 2026-11-06.** Playwright 1.64.1, Chromium 1234 headless, port 5108.

- **`test.fail` hides every thrown error, not every failure.** In the installed source `test.fail` sets `expectedStatus = "failed"`, and a result counts as expected only when its status equals that (`computeTestCaseOutcome`, `lib/runner/index.js`). `_failWithError` records `timedOut` for the test's own timeout and `failed` for anything else. So o1, the never-ready page with no bounded wait, hung at `locator.focus` for 60 s and was reported as a failure even under `test.fail`. A bounded wait that times out throws `TimeoutError`, which is `failed`, and o1b passed. VI-gates' "a timeout included" holds for a wait's or an action's own timeout, and not for the test's.
- **The old control's received value could not say which way focus left.** Both injected buttons had the class `outside`, so every run read `Received: "outside"` (o0, and every earlier `error-context.md` on disk). With them named apart, the blocked Tab lands on `outside-end`, the button after the UI. That is the browser's default move past the dialog's last control.
- **A wrong state is the broken fixture most likely to pass a naive replacement.** Under o2, PLAY for CRUISE, the card is not shown, so `focus()` on `reduced` changed nothing, and the Tab landed on II (`Received: "menu-button"`). Why II and not the first outside button was not examined. A replacement that asserted only that focus was not in the dialog would have passed on that page, which has no dialog [I: not run]. Hence the fixture assertions, and the shared check's own `reduced` assertion before the Tab, which n5 shows the control tells apart from the wrap.

**Found in the FH planning probes, 2026-11-07.** Throwaway scripts in the scratchpad `T08-fs/probe`, not project code and not acceptance. Each ran from that folder as `node ../../tools/gpu-lock.mjs T08 "node <script>"`, against `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js --port 5128 --strictPort --host 127.0.0.1` started from the repo, with the cached Chromium 1234 (it reports 151.0.7922.34) on the `gpu` project's flags, headless. Logs sit beside the scripts. The browser is one desktop Chromium; phones, tablets and other browsers were not run.

- **P1, fullscreen API** (`probe-fs.mjs`, 15:36:37Z to 15:36:52Z, exit 0, on `dev/ui.html?mount=0` with an injected checkbox whose `change` handler requests or exits) [O]:
  - `fullscreenEnabled` and `webkitFullscreenEnabled` are both true, and both request functions exist, at 1920x1080 and in touch contexts at 390x844 and 1024x768.
  - A request from the `change` handler resolved after a mouse click, a touch tap and Space, with `navigator.userActivation.isActive` true in the handler each time, and `fullscreenElement` was `<html>`.
  - The promise resolved before `fullscreenchange` fired: at 84 ms and 213 ms on desktop, 51 ms and 55 ms on the phone context. So the box follows the event, not the promise.
  - The viewport did not change in fullscreen: `innerWidth`, `innerHeight` and `screen` stayed at the emulated size, and `(display-mode: fullscreen)` stayed false.
  - A synthetic Escape in fullscreen reached the page (`keydown Escape fsEl=HTML trusted=true`) and did **not** leave fullscreen. So headless cannot show what a browser's own Esc does (HC11).
  - A page-script `document.exitFullscreen()` fired `fullscreenchange` with `fullscreenElement` `null`, and the probe's unwired box stayed ticked: the mirror is needed.
  - With no activation, a request from a timer was refused: `TypeError: Permissions check failed`, and a `fullscreenerror` event.
  - **Only the event of the API used fires.** With listeners for both events, a standard request logged only `fullscreenchange`; with the standard members removed, `webkitRequestFullscreen()` returned `undefined` and only `webkitfullscreenchange` fired. So the box listens to both.
- **P2, room in the card** (`probe-card.mjs card`, 15:37:19Z to 15:37:32Z, exit 0, `card.json`): `dev/ui.html?state=cruise` and `cruise-max` at A9's 13 sizes plus 768x1024, 1024x768 and 1280x720, with each candidate injected into the probe page's DOM as clones of the REDUCED MOTION row [O]:

  - L1, two full rows between QUALITY and REDUCED MOTION: scrolls at 844x390 by 32 px, 740x360 by 62, 667x375 by 47, 915x412 by 10.
  - L2, one row of two between QUALITY and REDUCED MOTION: scrolls at 740x360 by 16 and 667x375 by 1.

At 390x844, DPR 3, touch: 390x844 with a 585x1266 buffer, then 844x390 with 1266x585. The dev page followed the same way. No console error.

- **Read in installed source** [O]: Playwright 1.64.1's aria snapshot matcher (`matchesNode` in `node_modules/playwright-core/lib/coreBundle.js`) compares children with `containsList`, an in-order subsequence, unless the template sets `/children: equal` or `deep-equal`. So the hand-written cruise snapshot would still pass with rows added, and a hidden-HUD snapshot of II alone would also pass against a full HUD unless it says `/children: equal`. T10's `togglePause` (`src/app/commands.ts`) reads `d.sim.state.mode` when called and ~~queues `pause` or `resume`~~ **FH-V1:** calls `d.sim.command('pause')` or `('resume')`, which applies at once, so the live state, which `FrameView.sim` is, has changed when the call returns [O: `src/sim/sim.ts`, `src/sim/rules.ts`, read-only; the verifier found what this misreading caused, FH-36]. `vite.config.ts` binds `preview` to 127.0.0.1 and names `cacheDir` from `TERRAIN_PORT`, else `--port`, else 5100. TypeScript 6.0.3's `lib.dom.d.ts` types only the standard fullscreen API.

**Found in the FH review round 1 probes, 2026-11-07.** These are throwaway scripts in the scratchpad `T08-fs/probe`, with logs beside them. They are not project code and not acceptance. Same setup as P1 to P4: run as `node <scratchpad>/tools/gpu-lock.mjs T08 "node <script>"` against my own dev server on 127.0.0.1:5128, headless Chromium 1234 (151.0.7922.34) on the `gpu` project's flags. The browser is one desktop Chromium.

- **P5, order and errors** (`probe-fs2.mjs`, 16:17:46Z to 16:17:59Z, exit 0, on `dev/ui.html?mount=0` with an injected box, and listeners for `console` and `pageerror` that P1 lacked) [O]:
  - **A, exit order, 5 repeats:** a page-script `document.exitFullscreen()` resolved 0.2 to 0.4 ms after the call, with `fullscreenElement` already `null` and the change count not yet moved; `fullscreenchange` fired 0.4 to 0.6 ms after that. The order matches the reviewer's reading of the spec. In all 5 repeats a Playwright `waitForFunction(() => document.fullscreenElement === null, { polling: 1 })` returned after the event, because one round trip outlasted the gap. So a wait on the element alone held here by timing, not by order, which is why FH-27 waits on the count.
  - **Request order:** each request resolved with `fullscreenElement` already `<html>`, 0.3 to 142 ms before `fullscreenchange`. Re-reading the box when the promise settles therefore gives the real state.
  - **B and E, refusal with no activation** (a timer at 1 s, no evaluate before 2.5 s): the promise rejected (`TypeError: Permissions check failed`) about 0.1 ms before `fullscreenerror` fired. The console showed one `warning`, `Failed to execute 'requestFullscreen' on 'Element': API can only be initiated by a user gesture.`, besides Vite's two `debug` lines, and no `error`. With the rejection unhandled, `pageerror: TypeError: Permissions check failed` arrived as well. So T00's fixture passes a handled refusal and fails an unhandled one.
  - **C, exit with no fullscreen element:** `document.exitFullscreen()` rejected with `TypeError: Failed to execute 'exitFullscreen' on 'Document': Document not active`. Unhandled, it arrived as a `pageerror` with that text. `document.webkitExitFullscreen()` returned `undefined` and threw nothing.
  - **D, the gap, 3 repeats:** a script click on the ticked box, made inside a page-script exit's `then`, found `fullscreenElement` `null` with the count not yet moved; the handler's `exitFullscreen()` rejected with C's `TypeError`, and the change event followed 0.4 to 0.5 ms later. The gap is real and can be reached on purpose, so A30 (12) can test it without a stub.
- **P6, the label engine** (`probe-label.mjs`, 16:18:29Z to 16:18:30Z, exit 0, on `dev/ui.html?state=cruise` at 1920x1080, with a FULLSCREEN row cloned from REDUCED MOTION's label) [O]:

  - With `hidden` plus `.mv-ui [hidden] { display: none !important; }`: computed `display: none`, 0x0. Yet `getByLabel` still counted 1 (`exact: true` also 1), while `getByRole` counted 0, `getByLabel(...).filter({ visible: true })` 0, `isHidden()` true, and `getByRole(..., { includeHidden: true })` 1. This confirms the reviewer's reading of the label engine by a run.

The widest text was 92 px at 320 wide against the narrowest label of 127; at 1024x768 the labels were 128 px. The control failed 4 of 4: `toggle clip` for FULLSCREEN (text to 120.7 in a label ending at 111.4), `toggle overlap` of FULLSCREEN's text on HIDE HUD's label, and, with `nowrap`, `toggle clip` for REDUCED MOTION (to 326.3 in a label ending at 290.5) or, without it, `toggle wrap` (2 lines). The control's card scrolled 6 px against the planned row's 50, so the defect shortens the card, and the existing checks cannot see it.

**Found while executing FH, 2026-11-07.** Same machine, cached Chromium 1234 headless on the `gpu` project's flags.

- **A9's last-control check under-scrolled once the card grew** [O: `T08-fs/probe/probe-last.log`]. At `phone-small` the card is 596 in 546. Scrolling the 15 px REDUCED MOTION box with `block: 'nearest'` left its 44 px label at 528 to 572 against `innerHeight` 568, though the label scrolls fully into view (513 to 557 when the label itself is scrolled; 497 to 541 at the card's maximum scroll). FH-32 fixes the row. The check, in either form, does not catch a card that cannot scroll: `scrollIntoView` scrolls programmatically under `overflow: hidden` too [O: `probe-last2.log`].
- **The prefixed fullscreen calls change state after they return** [O: `T08-fs/flicker-before.log`]. Straight after a click that called `webkitRequestFullscreen()`, `webkitFullscreenElement` already read `<html>`, the count had not moved, and a box re-read on the settled promise read `false`. A read on settle is only sound for the standard promise (FH-35). Chromium sets both `fullscreenElement` and `webkitFullscreenElement` in standard fullscreen (every `review: FH` tick printed `"std":"HTML","webkit":"HTML"`).
- **`MV_READY` is logged only under `?harness`** [O: `src/app/boot.ts` lines 171 to 188, read-only], so A31 opens `/?harness` (FH-33).
- **A32's control does not separate at 33 ms on this machine** [O: `T08-fs/pa2/fh-firstshow-r1..r8.log`]: the planned control's untick read 31.1 to 42.4 ms over 5 runs, a layerless control 32.4 to 43.9 ms, while the row's own untick read 6.4 to 12.4 ms in 9 of 10 runs and 37.7 ms once (FH-34). In each of those 10 paired runs, and in the final one (12.2 against 32.0 ms), the control's largest untick frame was above the row's, so the cost D-012 removed does show; the margin around 33 ms is what is too thin for a gate.
- **The touch tap highlight shows on the toggle labels** [I: from the images]. In every touch image of the ticked card a light-blue band covers the HIDE HUD (or, on the app phone image, FULLSCREEN) cell just tapped. `ui.css` sets `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on buttons only, so every checkbox label in the card, MUTE and REDUCED MOTION included, gets the browser's default band when tapped [O: `ui.css`, the `:where(.mv-ui, .mv-fatal) button` rule]. It predates FH. Changing it is a look change, so it is HC13's question, not done here.
- **The app's phone buffer now reads 780x1688 at 390x844 DPR 3**, where P3 read 585x1266 [O: A31's log line in `T08-fs/fh-review-app2.log`]; after the rotation 1688x780. The aspect matches the viewport in both, which is what A31 checks. The ratio is T10's and T06's, not T08's; noted, not investigated.
- **Every `review: FH` image at DPR 2 or 3 came out at CSS size** with `page.screenshot({ scale: 'css' })` (390x844, 844x390, 768x1024, 1024x768, 320x568) [O: PNG headers read], so FH-23's helper keeps them under 1920 px.

**Found in the FH-V1 repair, 2026-11-07.** Same machine, cached Chromium 1234 headless on the `gpu` project's flags.

- **A command called inside `ui.frame` changes the frame being drawn, on the app but not on the dev page** [O]. On the app `FrameView.sim` is the sim's live state and `sim.command` applies at once (section 10's FH-V1 correction above). FH-8's `togglePause` therefore set autopilot on, and mode CRUISE, in the middle of a frame that had already read mode PLAY. That frame derived `hud auto`, drew the AUTOPILOT tag and announced it. The dev page's `togglePause` changed only the mode it would script for the next frame, so every FH row saw a clean PLAY-to-CRUISE change and could not catch this. On the unfixed code the dev row, once the dev page models the sim (FH-37), read `hud auto`, `play`, `AUTOPILOT - ANY INPUT RETAKES` and focus on body for the first frame after the exit, and the app recorder read the tag view and the announcement in batch 5, the mode in 6 and the card in 7 [O: `T08-fs/fv1/fb-dev.log`, `fb-app.log`]. The root cause was a misreading in this plan: "queues a command" (sections 4 and 10, corrected in place).
- **Dev-page screenshots are not byte-stable between runs** [O]. On identical source, FH's `fh-review` and `fh-full` runs differed in 7 of 28 `review: FH` images (`fs-shots` against `fh-full`, by `cmp`). So a byte comparison cannot show whether a change moved an image; a view or a pixel measure has to.

### Amendment LH

- Two existing rows depended on the title having exactly one focusable control and one prompt span, which LH removes: the `firstshow: panels` Tab check (`warmProblems` pressed Tab once from the focused launch button) and the title contrast row (`.mv-launch > .mv-if-fine`). The full run caught both: `Expected: true, Received: false` at the control's `Tab reaches the hidden` line, and `getComputedStyle ... parameter 1 is not of type 'Element'` at the contrast row. The guarded firstshow row itself kept passing, but its Tab check had become one that could not fail.

### Amendment WM

- `-webkit-text-stroke` is drawn along each contour of a glyph, not its filled outline, so a font that overlaps contours shows the joins. Arial Black, on the Windows machine where every earlier screenshot was taken, has none, which is why no viewed screenshot showed it. Bahnschrift, a variable font, reproduces it on Windows.
- Chrome's dark theme for sites darkens a page's light colours unless the page declares a dark `color-scheme` and the user prefers dark. The UI declared none, so the wordmark's light gradient stops were darkened to teal and black. The first phone screenshot already showed it, before WM's rim change. Headless test browsers never enable it, so no row could see it.

## 11. Decision log

| Decision | Rationale | Date | Author |
|---|---|---|---|
| Esc, P and M handled by `src/ui`; steering and boost by `src/input` | `ControlFrame` has no menu field, §3.2 forbids an import between them, and `UiCommands` already carries those actions. Key sets are disjoint | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| On-screen boost is rendered by UI and read by input through a delegated `data-mv-boost` attribute | Only UI knows the mode, so only it can hide the button. Only input can set `boost`. A DOM attribute keeps §3.2 intact, A3 proves both sides, and a contract constant would add nothing for two files with one owner | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Touch drag emits `absolute: true` from a virtual target | `InputSource` cannot see the ship and `sample` receives no `dt`, so a rate form would need T02's maximum rate and a clock. Cost: a stale target after autopilot causes a bounded glide, the same as a resting mouse | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| A released steer key gives `absolute: false, 0, 0`, not the last mouse target | Otherwise keyboard play flies back to wherever the mouse last sat | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Mouse maps the central 80% to the full window | Reaching full deflection should not need the screen edge. Unplaytested [A] | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Launch on `pointerup`, `click` and qualifying `keydown`; never `pointerdown` | Measured table in section 10 | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Tab, M and P do not launch, although Tab and M grant activation | Tab must stay navigation for keyboard users. M and P have their own meaning | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Panels derive only from `sim.mode`, `autopilot` and the lost flag | No second state machine to drift from §7.2 | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Stylesheet as `?inline` in one `<style>` | No extra request, so the fatal page cannot lose its styling. Verified through a build | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Wordmark uses a heavy system stack; HUD keeps §7.5's mono stack | §7.5's binding rule is system fonts only. Thin Courier strokes carry the chrome band poorly (section 10). One CSS token, so the operator or a reviewer can reverse it. §7.5 states this (DI-30) | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Upright glyphs plus `skewX`, never `italic` | Unfilled glyph edge otherwise | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| HUD is a CSS grid with named areas | Overlap becomes structurally unlikely, and A9 still checks it | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| HUD inside a central 16:9 box on wider screens | Keeps readouts in the eye's reach on 21:9 and 32:9. Viewed at 3440x1440 | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Panel alpha .8, accent colours lifted to luminance .40 | Measured contrast. A .62 to .42 gradient failed badly on white | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| HUD tints follow the zone's grid and accent, quantised to 16 steps | A HUD that follows the palette feels part of the world, and costs 32 writes per crossfade. Grid and accent are a contrasting pair in all six §6.8 rows | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Every slot throttled to 50 ms, leading-edge, not only text | Only text was throttled, so an all-fields-changing frame reached 580 writes per second against §7.5's 300. Excluding palette and beat from the adversarial case would have hidden a real cost; leading-edge keeps the beat kick on time in normal play (DI-50) | 2026-11-05 | T00 owner |
| Text slots throttled to 50 ms; overdrive 128 levels with prebuilt strings | Bounds the worst case, and keeps bars free of allocation and template strings. Imperceptible for score | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Shard events make no popup | At 12.5 per second they would dominate the budget, and the score ticking already says it | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Beat kick by flipping between two identical keyframe classes | One write per beat rather than one per frame, with no forced reflow | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Native range, checkbox and radio controls, and a hand-built dialog rather than `<dialog>` | Native controls give roles and arrows. Menu visibility must follow `sim.mode`, and browsers can close a `<dialog>` on repeated Esc regardless of `cancel` [M], which would desync it | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Bests and settings persistence live in `src/ui/storage.ts` | §7.1 names no owner; `sim` must stay pure | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Run km measured from launch; warps disqualify bests | DEMO can run for hours, and harness warps must not poison a real best | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| T08 alone reads `prefers-reduced-motion` | §10.3 names no owner, and `UiCommands.setReducedMotion` already carries it | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| DOM write gate is an observer plus a `setProperty` wrapper, and all style goes through `setProperty` | The observer alone misses same-value style writes | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Allocation gate is the sampling profiler, not a heap delta | A delta passed a real allocator | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| `src/ui/devPage.ts` holds dev states, injected with implementations | §12 gives T08 only `dev/ui.html`, and inline scripts are not type-checked. Injection keeps lint boundaries, and `index.ts` never imports it, so it does not ship | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| CSS backdrop rather than the real renderer in the dev page | T07 and T08 pages stay three-free per T00. The white backdrop is the worst case, and T10 and T11 check the real scene | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| No reticle; cursor hidden over the canvas during play | The ship is the feedback, and a reticle would add a per-frame write. Revisit at playtest | 2026-11-05 | T08 planner |
| Text slots take a format function and attribute slots a string table: `createTextSlot(target, format, minTicks)`, `createAttrSlot(target, name, table, minTicks)`. A `createGate(minTicks)` underneath is used directly where a key is not the value shown | M3's signatures named no format and no table, and comparing a number first needs both. Small course change, inside owned paths | 2026-11-05 | T08 executor |
| Stricter minimums on the same throttle: `aria-valuenow` 60 ticks, banner, grade and live region 60 ticks, popup starts 12 ticks | DI-50's 6 ticks is a ceiling on rate, so longer is allowed. It takes adversarial from 288 to 270 and cruise from 23 to 19.2. A popup that arrives inside the spacing is held, not dropped, and when two compete the more important one is kept | 2026-11-05 | T08 executor |
| Panels are one `data-view` attribute on `root`, with a 64-entry prebuilt table, read by CSS `~=` | One write per panel change rather than up to six, and the mask stays the single source (DI-50) | 2026-11-05 | T08 executor |
| Menu focus and `inert` follow the mask when it is actually written, not the mode | The panel write is throttled, and focusing a `display: none` element silently fails | 2026-11-05 | T08 executor |
| Run distance crosses calls in a `Float64Array(1)`, and `BestsTracker.update` takes that array | A7: a fractional double passed between closures allocates 12 B per call | 2026-11-05 | T08 executor |
| `formatSector(i, palette, maxChars = Infinity)`, with the HUD passing 19 under 360 px wide by `matchMedia` | M5's "drop the palette name" needs either a second text node, which adds to the write budget, or a width read, which A5g bans. Nineteen is what fits at the 11 px floor [arithmetic, then A9] | 2026-11-05 | T08 executor |

| FH-26 (FH-R1, finding 6). No fullscreen rejection leaves `fullscreen.ts`. Request and exit each catch the browser's promise and return one that always resolves. Exit is called only while either property holds an element; with none, the box is simply re-read. The own-exit mark is set only when an exit is called, cleared if that exit rejects, and consumed by the next change that finds no element. Those three steps are pure functions over a small record in `fullscreen.ts`, so U8 can test them | Under the spec, and in Chromium 1234, `exitFullscreen()` with no fullscreen element rejects with a `TypeError`, and unhandled it is a `pageerror` [O: P5 C]. Unhandled, it would fail T00's fixture and be an unhandled rejection in production. The guard keeps the browser from being asked; the catch covers any other rejection, such as a document that is no longer fully active [M]. | FH-27 (FH-R1, finding 2). Every FH fullscreen test (A30, A31, A41's smoke) counts the four change and error events with an init-script listener and waits for the count to move before it steps a frame or reads the box or `fullscreenElement`. **FH-R2 (3):** It waits only after an action expected to fire one of those events. ~~After an action that must leave fullscreen unchanged, it reads the count unchanged and `fullscreenElement` still `<html>` instead, as A30's header states~~ **FH-R3 (1) (v):** The no-change read applies only to an action taken in fullscreen, once it has settled, that must leave fullscreen unchanged. After such an action the test reads the count unchanged from its 'before' value and `fullscreenElement` still `<html>`. It reads them right after the action, and after a following `step()` only when that step comes before the next fullscreen action. Every 'before' count is taken only after the previous fullscreen action's wait has finished; A30 (5)'s is 0 by construction, from its collected console lines (FH-R3 (2)). An action taken inside a fullscreen action's wait, as A30 (8)'s Resume straight after the untick, is read under that wait: the count up by one and `fullscreenElement` `null`. An action outside fullscreen gets no such read. A41's HIDE HUD tick and reload are such actions, since they come after its page-script exit. The rule is limited to fullscreen rather than written as "`fullscreenElement` as it was before the action", because a reload restarts the counter, so a count read across one would fail correct code [I: page state goes with the reload, and the init script runs again (FH-31)]. The details are in A30's header | An exit settles its promise with `fullscreenElement` already `null`, and the event fires at a later rendering step [O: P5 A, 0.4 to 0.6 ms later here; P1 saw 3 ms]. A wait on the element alone can therefore return before the product has seen the event. Here it did not in 5 of 5, but only because a Playwright round trip outlasted the gap, which a busier machine need not repeat [I]. The leave flag and the own-exit mark both change on that event, so without the count, A30 (8)'s PLAY-exit case could fail correct code, and its untick-then-Resume case could miss f3. ~~The counter is installed before page scripts, so its listener runs first in the same dispatch, and by the time the count has moved the product has handled the event.~~ **FH-R2 (3):** By the time the count has moved, the product has handled the event. Listener order does not give that. It holds because the dispatch is synchronous, so no `waitForFunction` poll, `evaluate` or console-driven read can run between the counter's listener and the product's [I: from the DOM's synchronous dispatch, not probed]. A30 (5) waits on the counter's console line, because a `waitForFunction` would grant activation [O: `Agents.md`] | 2026-11-07 | T08 executor |

**Amendment LH decisions** (2026-11-11, T08 executor, operator-approved in session):

- **LH-1.** Two buttons replace the prompt button: LAUNCH (`data-mv="launch"`, HUD shown) and LAUNCH - NO HUD (`data-mv="launch-nohud"`, HUD hidden). Labels are the operator's. LAUNCH keeps the `launch` hook so other tasks' tests keep clicking the same thing.
- **LH-2.** Both buttons write FH's `hideHud` setting through the menu: the HIDE HUD box, the root's `data-hide-hud` and storage, only when the value changes. No new setting and no storage change, so the title and the CRUISE box always agree, and a stored choice survives a reload.
- **LH-3.** Any launch key and a tap or click on the title away from the buttons still launch, with the remembered setting (operator's answer). A key pressed while a start button has focus launches with that button's choice; a pointer on a button launches with its choice. The choice is applied only when the launch itself is allowed (DEMO, not fatal, boot running).
- **LH-4.** At mount focus goes to the button matching the stored setting, so a key alone repeats the remembered choice and the A24 ring rule is unchanged.
- **LH-5.** `PROMPT_KEY` and `PROMPT_TOUCH` leave `copy.ts`, as nothing reads them; the button labels name the action. `PROMPT_LOADING` stays.
- **LH-6.** While loading (DI-95) LAUNCH reads LOADING as before, and LAUNCH - NO HUD keeps its label in the same muted loading style. Both stay focusable, so LH-4 works during loading, and the existing `bootReady` guard stops both.
- **LH-7.** The pair sits in one `.mv-launches` row that wraps on a narrow screen; LAUNCH - NO HUD is drawn smaller, as the secondary choice. The attract fade keeps the row (selector `:not(.mv-launches)`).
- **E5.** Expected-output edits, no threshold moved: rows that found the button by the name PRESS ANY KEY or TAP TO LAUNCH now find LAUNCH with an exact name match; the cold-load row expects the prompt LAUNCH.
- **E6.** Two fixture edits found by A47, no threshold moved. `warmProblems` focuses `[data-mv="launch-nohud"]`, the title's last focusable control, before its Tab, as it once tabbed from the only one; the control shows the check fails when the panels are exposed. The title contrast row measures both buttons' labels in place of the removed prompt span, so it now covers two elements where it covered one.
- **WM-1.** The wordmark's white rim is four `drop-shadow`s offset `.006em` left, right, up and down, at the stroke's colour `rgba(255,255,255,.75)`, on each `.mv-wm`. A drop-shadow follows the rendered alpha, so it traces only the letter's silhouette. `.006em` is the outer half of the old `.012em` centred stroke. Rejected: `paint-order` (the fill is transparent, so the stroke still shows); `text-shadow` (painted over a `background-clip: text` fill); dropping the rim (the operator chose to keep it).
- **WM-2.** Under `forced-colors` the rim filter is off, as the stroke was, so the wordmark is plain `CanvasText`.
- **WM-3.** `color-scheme: dark` on `.mv-ui, .mv-fatal`, the UI's own scope, says the UI is dark by design, so Chrome's dark theme for sites leaves its colours alone. On `.mv-ui` it is enough (`WM/g-runtime-ui.png`), so no rule on `html` and no edit to T10's `index.html`. Its other effect is dark native scrollbars; every control already has `appearance: none` outside `forced-colors`. Rejected: `color-scheme: only light` (did not stop forced dark in this browser, and would declare a light page).

## 12. Contract change requests

All additive, comments or new constants only, and all reconciled. The text below is a record of what was asked, and the fallbacks are removed.

**CCR-T08-1. Meaning of `ControlFrame`. Approved, C-15; absorbed into architecture.md §8 on 2026-11-05; §8's wording wins.** T02 and T08 must agree, and the type says nothing about units or signs. `src/contracts/index.ts`, directly above `export interface ControlFrame`:

```ts
/**
 * One sample per rendered frame (§3.3 step 3); the sim reuses it for every step of that frame.
 * absolute false: moveX, moveY are steering rates in [-1, 1].
 * absolute true: moveX, moveY are a target in [-1, 1] across the play window, x = moveX * PLAY_HALF_X,
 *   y = PLAY_Y_MIN + (moveY + 1) / 2 * (PLAY_Y_MAX - PLAY_Y_MIN). The sim approaches it at its own bounded rate.
 * +moveX is sim +x and screen right. +moveY is sim +y and screen up.
 * boost: held this frame. human: a device event arrived since the last sample, or a key, button or finger is held.
 */
```

**CCR-T08-2. Shield and SYNC limits. Approved, C-16; absorbed into architecture.md §8 on 2026-11-05; §8's wording wins.** The HUD must draw a fixed number of pips, and T02's constants are in a module the UI may not import. §7.1 gives the SYNC cap, and T00's seam table says shields 0 to 3. `src/contracts/index.ts`, after `EVENT_CAPACITY`:

```ts
export const SHIELD_MAX = 3;           // SimState.shields is an integer in [0, SHIELD_MAX]
export const SYNC_MAX = 16;            // SimState.sync is an integer in [1, SYNC_MAX]
```

**CCR-T08-3. Event and objective field meanings. First comment amended, C-17. Second comment amended, C-12: merged into `ZoneState` in T01's wording, so T08's separate comment above `ZoneState` is dropped. Absorbed into architecture.md §8 on 2026-11-05; §8's wording wins.** §8.1 lists the `EventBuffer` fields without saying what `value` and `index` hold for each kind, and `objectiveTarget` has no unit. `src/contracts/index.ts`, directly above `export const Ev`:

```ts
/**
 * EventBuffer fields. x, y: ship position in u when the event fired.
 * value: SHARD, GATE, GRAZE points added including multipliers; HIT shields remaining; SECTOR new sector index; GRADE bonus points; others 0.
 * index: GRADE rank, 0 S, 1 A, 2 B, 3 C; otherwise the item's index in its row, or 0 if none.
 * chunk: the item's chunk, or the ship's chunk if there is no item.
 */
```

and directly above `export interface ZoneState`:

```ts
/** objectiveTarget and SimState.objectiveProgress share a unit: a count for GATES, CHAIN, GRAZES; u for NO_HIT, BOOST. */
```

**CCR-T08-4. Palette and biome display names and order. Approved, C-11; absorbed into architecture.md §8 on 2026-11-05; §8's wording wins.** The HUD names them, `src/world` is off limits to the UI, and the index order is otherwise only implied by table rows. `src/contracts/index.ts`, after `BIOME_COUNT`:

```ts
/** Display names. Index order is the rows of §6.8 and §5.4; T01's tables follow it. */
export const PALETTE_NAMES = ['Sunset Drive', 'Miami Dusk', 'Ultraviolet', 'Toxic Arcade', 'Ember', 'Arctic Laser'] as const;
export const BIOME_NAMES = ['Boulevard', 'Neon City', 'Crystal Desert', 'Cyber Canyon', 'Orbital Night', 'Olympus'] as const;
```

**CCR-T08-5. When `UiCommands` are called. Amended, C-25; absorbed into architecture.md §8 on 2026-11-05; §8's wording wins.** T10 must route them correctly without reading this plan. `src/contracts/index.ts`, directly above `export interface UiCommands`:

```ts
/**
 * launch runs synchronously inside an activating event and may fire more than once for one gesture:
 *   it must unlock audio in the same call stack, be idempotent, and do nothing to the sim outside DEMO.
 * togglePause: PLAY to CRUISE, CRUISE to PLAY, otherwise nothing.
 * Ui.mount calls setVolume and setReducedMotion at once, so their targets must exist before mount.
 * Ui is the only reader of prefers-reduced-motion. Ui.showFatal may be called before mount.
 */
```

The executor filed no new request and needed no amendment.

**Amendment FH: none.** `Ui`, `UiCommands`, `FrameView` and every other §8 type are unchanged. The two new DOM hooks, the root attribute and the `hideHud` field are T08's own, additive like `mute-toggle` (section 11). The contract's list of DOM hooks (`src/contracts/index.ts`, above `interface Ui`) names the hooks other tasks rely on; no other task needs the new ones, so it is not asked to change. What `architecture.md` says about the cruise menu and §7.2's transitions is for the T00 owner to amend (section 13, FH).

## 13. Outcomes and retrospective

**Result against Purpose, 2026-11-05.**

1. Title: met. Chrome wordmark from system fonts and CSS, prompt, controls, bests. Launch runs synchronously inside the first real gesture, proven with no evaluate before it, on mouse, key, touch tap and touch drag.
2. HUD: met at all nine sizes by A9, and viewed at each.
3. Cruise menu: met. Keyboard-only path run end to end, mouse and touch through real events. It states that the road never stops and why there is no true pause.
4. Overlays and fatal: met. All four fatal reasons opaque by a pixel probe, and working with no mount.
5. Controls: met per §7.3 against the source table, in Node and with real browser events.
6. Frame cost: met. Measured, on this machine, with siblings running.
7. Accessibility basics: met. Roles and names by aria snapshot, focus trap with a working negative control, 44 px targets, measured contrast at 5.06 or better over white and 8.45 or better over the scene, no animation under reduced motion.

**Ran and passed:** A0 to A18n, every row except A19. Evidence is in section 9. 171 unit tests, 189 browser tests, one pass, exit 0.

**Not run:** A19, `npm run check`, which waits for the batch. `npm run typecheck`, `lint`, `test` and `test:browser` were not run either, per the batch rules; `check:task` covers them for owned paths.

**Not verified, human checks, open:**

- HC1. Layout, touch and targets on real phones and tablets. All of it here is emulated.
- HC2. Whether iOS Safari's activation starts audio at launch.
- HC3. Android and iOS font fallback for the wordmark and HUD.

- Score above about 1e9 stops being a small integer, so passing it between closures will box it, 12 B per call and three calls per frame. Well inside §9.2's 128 B per frame, and not measured.

V1 named those in the problem, but its fix asked for the SYNC label and the chip only. **Review, closed at V2:** A11's content-box measuring was accepted by the V2-T08 verifier (section 11). **A7's margin:** the first sampled pass read up to 57,396 B against 64 KB on a busy machine; warm passes read 5.7 to 8.0 KB, so the gate's margin is warm-up noise, which could make it flaky under load. **Unverified:** `focusVisible` on engines other than Chromium 1234; the Resume ring after a real tap. A19 and the human checks stay as above. Per DI-64, T10 may count T08 as done only after the post-repair whole-repo run is green.

**R2 repair, against V2-T08's one finding.** A13's four fatal-reason tests went from 32.0 to 32.4 s each (V2: up to 52.6 s under load) to 0.41 to 0.73 s, against the 60 s timeout, with the same shot, criterion and threshold, and two added assertions. Each test fails when its own reason's page lets the stage through, and the old and new checks give the same counts. The whole spec now runs in 2.1 min, not 4.3 to 5.7. Not verified: A19 and `test:browser`, still per DI-64; the times were taken with siblings running, at CPU spot readings of 6 to 57%.

It draws the HUD, and the menu card, invisibly during DEMO, so a fresh browser pays its one-time cost before the click. T10's regression went from exit 1 (34.1 ms) to exit 0 (6.4 and 12.2 ms) on the app, and the new A20 row from 57.3 ms to 6.4 to 12.3 ms on the dev page, with an old-path control at 58.5 to 72.9 ms. The menu's first open on the app went from 28.7 to 36.2 ms to 7.2 to 12.1 ms. Every section 7 browser row passed after the change (220), A9's 111 and A11's numbers among them, A11's identical to R1's; T09's B12 and B13 and T10's context, mode, motion, touch and compile rows passed on the app (21).

- The cost of the warm layers themselves: GPU memory for the HUD and card tiles, and compositing two layers at .001 through DEMO. Not measured; on a phone it belongs with HC6.

3. **Popup contrast (A23).** Dark plates. Lowest p10 over the real scene across 4 held frames: **HIT 4.41**, MISS 7.90, GRAZE 10.77, the rest 13.98 or more (old: HIT 1.19). Over pure white, the worst case for these colours on this plate: HIT 3.89, all others 5.76 or more. The plate-less control fails on the same frames, and m4 fails over white.

6. **DI-95 (A26).** On a cold load of the app, a Space pressed as soon as the title exists (371 to 576 ms in; the dev server mounts it too late for 200 ms) reads `LOADING`, launches nothing and builds no `AudioContext`. Once `running`, the prompt is back and a press launches. m7 shows that the CSS alone is not the fix.

Every earlier section 7 row still passes (235 in the final pass), with A5, A6, A8, A9 and A11 at the D4 numbers and A7 and A20 inside their limits. Unit tests: 204.

**For other owners, found here and not fixed, because the paths are theirs:**

- **T10, A19 in `tests/e2e/modes.spec.ts`,** "launch by key a; menu button opens CRUISE": it clicks II with a mouse on the `gpu` project in PLAY, which item 2 now forbids. It fails on the final code (`Expected: "cruise", Received: "play"`) and passed on the pre-repair source. That step needs Tab then Enter, or a touch context.
- **T10, A18n in `tests/e2e/motion.spec.ts`:** its floor clip includes T08's popup band, and popups animate on CSS while the loop is stopped, so a popup starting at the stop changes the clip, on the old CSS too (716 pixels at a 0 ms stop). It passes now because the plate's glow was dropped, but it still depends on timing. Hiding `#mv-ui` for the clip, reading the canvas, or waiting .9 s after the stop would remove that.
- **T10, A17b:** `src/app/boot.ts`'s outer catch still rethrows (VI-r1's blocking finding), so the row passes or fails by where the fault lands; it failed in one of my three runs on the final code.

**Not verified in the VI repair:**

- Whether a player on a hybrid laptop misses tapping II under a fine primary pointer; no such device was tried.
- How often overlapping popups, which now hide one another's plates, occur in real play; seen only with all six forced on.
- The cold load on a built `dist`, where VI-game's title mounted at 267 ms; only the dev server was run.
- `focusVisible` on engines other than Chromium 1234; iOS Safari, as before (HC2).
- This plan's own A19, the whole-repo `npm run check`, and the whole-repo `test:browser` and `test:software`, not run, per the batch rules; human checks HC1 to HC7 as before.

**FC repair, against VI-gates' finding on A10's control.** A10's negative control no longer uses `test.fail`. It runs the same wrap check as the row and passes only when that check fails at its wrap assertion, with expected `resume` and received `outside-end`, after one Tab stopped by the injected listener, on a page asserted ready in `cruise`. On the old spec, three of four broken fixtures were counted as the expected failure, exit 0: a never-ready page with a 5 s ready wait, a page in PLAY, and a fixture that threw. On the new spec, all of them fail, exit 1, each naming what broke, and so do two more cases: a listener that no longer stops the Tab, and a failure of the check's other assertion. The fourth old case, a never-ready page with no bounded wait, already failed under `test.fail`, after 60 s, because Playwright records the test's own timeout as `timedOut` (section 10). So the finding's "a timeout included" was true of bounded waits and not of that one. The full spec passed, 235 of 235 with no cross, and `check:task T08` passed.

**Not verified in the FC repair:**

- Other `test.fail`-style controls elsewhere in the repo. None remains in `ui.spec.ts` (`grep -c "test.fail("` 0), and other files are not T08's.
- Whether a future Playwright release changes how a caught `expect` is recorded. The control relies on 1.64.1's behaviour, read in the installed source and seen in the n-runs. A change that made caught failures fail the test would turn this control red, not green [I].
- This plan's own A19, the whole-repo `npm run check`, `test:browser` and `test:software`, not run, per the batch rules; human checks HC1 to HC7 as before.

**Lessons:**

- A mutation that survives is the row's fault until shown otherwise: m5 passed because the case started from the state the mutation could not change.
- A change inside my own paths can break a sibling's row for reasons in neither plan; running the siblings' rows on the old and new code was the only way to tell mine from theirs.
- A one-time cost needs a control that is truly cold. Restoring the old path after the page had drawn once looked like a pass, because the cost was already paid; only an override in place before any element existed showed it.
- A gate that measures a box measures whatever the box contains: the chip's own border failed it, and the old card checks skipped the card entirely. Both were found by giving the new row a control that had to fail.
- Viewing found what the gates could not: the button reset, doubled legends, the forced-colours cut, the wrong legend wording.
- The gates found what viewing could not: boxed doubles, glow lifting contrast, a floor that rounded down too far.
- Two of my own negative controls were vacuous when first written, the Tab wrap and the tap race. Each looked fine until the thing it guarded was switched off. Showing a control fail is what caught them, as `Agents.md` says.
- On this machine a dev server's reload can arrive long after an edit, so a green or red first test right after editing means little.
- A negative control has to name the failure it shows. "It failed" is satisfied by the fixture breaking, and `test.fail` cannot tell the two apart. Running the old control on deliberately broken fixtures was the quickest way to see that.

Lessons:

- A row that reads a box after an asynchronous action needs to know when the state it reads actually changed. The standard promise and the prefixed call differ, and only a mutation run (f6) showed the box flipping on the prefixed path.
- A gate whose control sits a few milliseconds past its limit is not a gate on a shared machine; several runs of the control, not one, decide it.
- My own test helpers had two bugs that read like product failures (a mis-sorted key list, frames sharing one tick). Each was found by reading the failure against the code before touching the code.
- **FH-V1:** a dev page is a model of the host, and where it differs from the host the suite cannot see. Here it applied commands a frame late, so no row could see an in-frame call change the frame being drawn. The plan had also described T10's call as "queues a command" without reading `sim.command`. A claim about another module's timing that a design rests on needs its source read, not inferred from the call site.
